The working thread

This is the student's own thread: the brief it was given, what it wrote, each step it took, and every image it opened, shown as it saw it (possibly downscaled). Some of these images were later overwritten in the study folder, so this is the only place they survive. The raw record is the transcript.

The brief

You are a student learning to paint one thing, in the painterly pixel-art language of Mark Ferrari: driftwood (whole trees and logs stranded on river gravel bars: silvered, barkless trunks with root wads and snapped branch stubs, piled against each other).

How you learn: build up, one form at a time, in stages. Don't move to the next stage until the current one is truly convincing to your own eye. Look at every image you make with the Read tool.

  1. One log, in greyscale: a cylinder lying on gravel, lit from several directions. Its light and shadow families, core shadow, the dark contact line, and its cast shadow on the stones. Then its ends: sawn, snapped and splintered. Sizes from 120 px long down to a few pixels.
  2. The wood's material: silvered, weathered grain running along the log, cracks (narrow forms, so drawn as clean pixel runs), knots, and the difference from fresh bark.
  3. Branch stubs and root wads: a root wad is a mass (a fringed, tangled disc), while stubs and roots are narrow strokes.
  4. Colour: silver-grey wood with warm and cool light; wet versus dry.
  5. A pile: logs crossing and resting on each other, separated by value, with the gravel and cobbles around and under them, and how a pile reads with distance.
  6. Sheets: your technique as code with parameters (length, diameter, orientation, root wad yes/no, weathering, wetness, light and hour, camera angle low or 30° oblique, distance, seed), swept on labelled sheets. Every cell should look painted.

At every stage, make side-by-sides: a photo crop | your pixels | the nearest thing in Ferrari's work, at 8×. His fallen or dead trunks and tree forms are in his scenes in ~/work/pixelart-studies/shared/refs and pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw/ (look for trunks, limbs, logs), and his rock rules are in master-copy/src/c5_boulder.py.

Photos:

  • ~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg;
  • more via python3 ~/work/pixelart-studies/tools/imgfetch.py "driftwood gravel bar river" -n 12 -o refs --sheet (also "driftwood log close", "root wad").

Background reading: what other students found about form and technique, in ~/work/pixelart-studies/SYNTHESIS.md and pedagogy/*/LESSONS.md.

Deliverables in ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/:

  • LESSONS.md: each stage with its images, what you learned, and an honest comparison;
  • the technique code;
  • the stage images;
  • the sheets. Read anywhere under ~/work/pixelart-studies/, write only in your folder. Resources: nice -n 10, at most 4 threads. Take the time mastery takes.

# output:
# Painter core: one method (draft 1)

Synthesized by the integrator from the four pedagogy arms (`pedagogy/{atelier,master-copy,analysis,observation}`) and, secondarily, the seven topic studies.

Each rule is tagged:

- **[shown]**: a student implemented it and it visibly works in their sheets.
- **[measured]**: a student measured it in Ferrari's pixels or in photos.
- **[mine]**: my inference, not yet built by anyone.

This isn't a committee of special strengths. The arms converged on one machine independently, and the draft describes that machine.

## The convergence

Working alone, all four arms landed on the same architecture:

- **Paint indices, not colours.**
  - Master copy found that Ferrari's index map is literally a form map: indices are bins of surface direction, and "the palette is the light". The same index image serves morning and afternoon. [measured]
  - Atelier paints in "step space" along one material ramp. [shown]
  - Sky found that two of Ferrari's hour pairs are identical pixels. [measured]
  - Analysis found that value is the structure and colour is temperature and distance. [measured]
- **Two light families that never touch.** Atelier's terminator rule [shown], analysis's three-value picture plan with every mass kept inside its group [measured], and rock's lit and shadow groups [shown] are the same idea at two scales.
- **Dither mixes only adjacent steps of one ramp.** All four arms use it. Ordered 2×2 dither suits hard or precise things (rock, sky seams, water rows); random or diffusion dither suits organic texture (lawns, foam). Master copy measured this [measured]; analysis cites Ferrari's GDC talk for the same split.
- **The medium's own geometry.** Contours are clean runs (1:1, 2:1, 4-4-4 flats), measured on Ferrari's crests by analysis and atelier [measured]. Atelier redraws every major contour by snapping edges to clean ratios [shown]. Masses meet level ground or water in a dead-flat run with a 1-px darker contact line [measured: analysis, observation].
- **Clean runs are for narrow forms; mass boundaries are where texture ends.** David's correction (2026-09-25): the clean-run rule came from the *grass strokes*. It applies to anything narrow, drawn as a line: blades, cracks, joints, twigs, conifer spires, stems, a snowline drawn as a stroke. A 1–2 px form must be a clean run of 1:1, 2:1 or 4-4-4 steps, or it reads as noise [measured: analysis, atelier; the runs they found on crests are this rule too, since a crest line against the sky is drawn as a stroke]. A *mass outline* isn't drawn as a line. A textured mass (grass mound, foliage, scrub, scree) ends in a fringe: blade tips break its top, dark holes open along the crest with lit peaks just behind, and the form turns through mark density [measured: master copy C2 and C3, "far crest has dark holes (40%)"; C4, "the silhouette against the water is not an edge but a fringe"]. **Failure seen:** master copy's final painter drew its hillock boundary as a clean contour and g

## 0. What the reference turned out to be, and the finding that set up the rest

The reference file is 1268×938. Run-length analysis shows it is an exact 2× upscale of a **634×469 native image with 172 colours**. It is Ferrari's **Mirror Pond** from *Living Worlds*, shown in a dusk palette.

The original indexed data of the colour-cycling scenes is public. I fetched it from the Canvas Cycle demo (`effectgames.com/demos/canvascycle/images/*.LBM.json`; `src/lw2png.py`) for these scenes: Mirror Pond (Morning V16, Afternoon V16PM), Mountain Stream (V19, V19PM), Mountain Fortress Dusk, Seascape Sunset, Pond Ripples, Forest Edge, Mountain Storm, Island Fires, Approaching Storm and Deep Forest. The reference aligns with V16PM at offset (3,4). 86% of its pixels map one-to-one onto V16PM indices; the rest are colour-cycled water.

**The finding: in both Mirror Pond and Mountain Stream, the morning and afternoon versions have identical index maps.** Only the palette differs. Light direction, time of day and colour temperature are all palette decisions. The picture itself, drawn in indices, is a *form map*: each index is a class of surface orientation within a material. On the Mirror Pond island, for example, four classes run across the mound. In the morning palette class 74 is yellow and lit. In the afternoon palette class 77 is the lit ochre and 74 becomes the far-side reflected light (`crops/p_island_am_pm.png`). In Mountain Stream the lit faces of the boulders flip from the tops (AM) to the right-facing planes (PM) without a single pixel changing (`crops/p_c5_am_pm.png`).

That gave me a method as well as a subject. For every copy I could read the index map directly: the class sequences, the transition statistics and the depth palettes. I did not have to guess them from RGB.

What Ferrari has said about his process (sources: the EffectGames Q&A with Mark J. Ferrari, and an interview in CGMagazine):

- He started dithering at Lucasfilm because 16 fixed colours were useless. A checkerboard of two colours "blurred into a solid third colour", "kind of like mixing paint".
- He chooses the dither by effect. He uses *patterned* dither when he wants a surface "smooth and precise", *diffusion* dither for chaotic things s

# output:
# Lessons from the atelier: a boulder by a tarn at dusk, after Mark Ferrari

I worked value first and colour last. Each stage below says what I learned, points to the images, and says what went wrong. After the stages come the method (the program) and an honest comparison of my sheets with Ferrari's tarn.

Everything is in `~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/atelier/`. To regenerate every sheet, run `nice -n 10 python3 method.py sheets`: four single-thread worker processes, about 4 minutes.

**Research, and its limits.** I studied Ferrari's `ferrari_alpine_tarn_dusk.png` closely:

- palette statistics;
- crops at his native pixel scale (the file is his 634×469 frame doubled);
- measured run lengths of his contours.

I read his Q&A (EffectGames) on:

- palette economy;
- patterned dither for smooth ramps and diffusion dither for chaos;
- colour cycling.

On form and light I used Proko's value families (the terminator, core, reflected light that never reaches the halftone, cast shadow, occlusion). I also read Pedro Medeiros and Slynyrd on clustering and on rocks, and the notes on Kazuo Oga's poster-colour method (big soft masses first, detail last, about 21 colours).

I did **not** look closely at Ferrari's other colour-cycling scenes or at Ghibli plates. Saving images from the web counts as a download, which needs permission I could not ask for in this session, so I only had text descriptions of them. That gap is real and shows in the results (see the comparison).

## Stage 1: light on simple forms (greyscale)

![forms](figs/s1_forms.png)
![low sun](figs/s1_lowsun.png)

The top row of the first sheet is the two-value **notan** (light family against shadow family). The bottom row is the six-value painting.

What I learned:

- **The two families must never touch.** Early versions let ambient occlusion push lit ground into the shadow family. The notan showed a black pool round every base, and the forms stopped reading. The fix is a rule, not a tweak: occlusion may dim the light family, but it only crosses into shadow at a thin contact line. I found the same bug a second time, in its SDF normalisation, when the terrain distance field was scaled. The notan caught it both times.
- **A value plan beats a lighting formula.** Mapping N·L linearly onto greys gave the lit face of the cube the same grey as the ground. What worked was a six-step plan:
  - light: steps 5 and 4;
  - halftone: 3;
  - *(the gap at the terminator)*;
  - reflected light: 2;
  - shadow: 1;
  - core, occlusion and the root of the cast shadow: 0.

  The greys are spaced by eye (0.07, 0.17, 0.27 | 0.52, 0.70, 0.90), with a big jump at the terminator. A *keyed* material like the ground gets its own local value (an albedo) so it turns fewer steps.
- **The core falls out of geometry.** Point the bounce light from below and away from the sun, and the band just past the terminator faces neither light nor bounce. The cylinder then shows light, halftone, terminator, a dark core, a reflected-li

"""tech_logs.py -- fallen trunks, driftwood, root plates: techniques for things Ferrari never painted.

Nearest Ferrari analogues: the V26 trunks (vertical fissure strokes, bark plates), the V02 dead tree
(1-px twig lines in clean runs, a dark mass with a lit edge).

  bark(ctx, log, mat)                trigger: a trunk >= ~3 px thick.  recipe: plates elongated ALONG
                                     the axis (a stretched noise in the log's own (along, around)
                                     coordinates) + dark fissure lines between them; lit rim on the
                                     sun-facing side of the silhouette; the underside one step darker
                                     and a contact line where it lies on the ground.
  bleached(ctx, log, mat)            trigger: driftwood.  recipe: no bark: long smooth pale planes
                                     with a few dark checks (cracks) along the axis; strong lit top /
                                     dark underside split.
  splinter(ctx, log, mat_wood)       trigger: the snapped top end of a trunk broken by the slide.
                                     recipe: the break is a fan of 1-px spikes of pale raw wood
                                     projecting along the axis, lengths ragged (0.2-1.0 x diameter),
                                     each spike a clean-run line, lit on the sun side; the break face
                                     a pale lozenge.  Pale raw wood is the brightest thing on the fan.
  thin_logs(ctx, logs, mat)          trigger: a trunk thinner than ~1 px.  recipe: a 1-px clean-run
                                     line along the projected axis (lit step if its top is lit) with a
                                     dark pixel under it -- the smallest light-top/dark-bottom pair.
  rootplate_marks(ctx, plate, mat, root_mat)   trigger: an upturned root plate >= ~4 px.
                                     recipe: the disc is dark soil (shadow family) broken by clods and
                                     embedded stones (specks); its rim is a fringe of clods and root
                                     ends; roots hang from the lower rim as 1-px lines (clean runs);
                                     the sun-side rim gets lit flecks; a black pit under it.
"""
import numpy as np
from noise import value, rand2
from tech_ground import line_pixels


def _log_mask(ctx, log):
    return (ctx.B.obj == log.oid) & (ctx.C.mat == log.mat)


def bark(ctx, log, mat, plate_m=0.6, fissure=0.18, amp=0.9):
    C = ctx.C; B = ctx.B; m = ctx.scene.materials[mat]

# output:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""imgfetch: fast, polite reference-image sourcing (Wikimedia Commons + Openverse).

  python3 imgfetch.py "cinder cone scoria slope" -n 12 -o refs/cinder
  python3 imgfetch.py -f queries.txt -n 8 -o refs            # one query per line; subdir per query
  options: --src commons,openverse   --width 1200   --sheet (writes <out>/_sheet.jpg)

Writes images plus <out>/sources.tsv (file, title, license, author, page url).
Cached: files already present are skipped. Handles 429/5xx with Retry-After backoff.
Parallel downloads (4 workers) with a shared per-host rate limiter (~3 req/s; Wikimedia's media
host is kept to 1 per 2 s and slows further on any 429, since it throttles hard per IP after bursts).
Openverse (mostly Flickr-hosted CC images) is the primary source because it stays fast.
"""
import argparse, concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, re, sys, threading, time, urllib.error, urllib.parse, urllib.request

UA = "pixelart-studies-imgfetch/1.0 (research; contact: local)"
_locks = {}; _last = {}; _glock = threading.Lock()

SLOW_HOSTS = {"upload.wikimedia.org": 2.0}   # Wikimedia media host: ~1 req/s or it 429s

def _throttle(host, min_gap=0.34):
    min_gap = SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, min_gap)
    with _glock:
        lk = _locks.setdefault(host, threading.Lock())
    with lk:
        dt = time.time() - _last.get(host, 0)
        if dt < min_gap:
            time.sleep(min_gap - dt)
        _last[host] = time.time()

def get(url, tries=4, timeout=25):
    host = urllib.parse.urlparse(url).netloc
    if host == "upload.wikimedia.org":
        tries = 2  # fail fast; other sources fill in
    for i in range(tries):
        _throttle(host)
        try:
            req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": UA})
            return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout).read()
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
  download failed: HTTP Error 429: Too many requests - please contact noc@wikimedia.org to discuss a less disruptive approach or instead use thumbnail images in sizes listed on https://w.wiki/GHai. (0c640b1)

Write: elements/driftwood/src/geo.py
"""geo.py -- the world the painter needs, and no more.

World: Z up, ground is the plane z = 0 (gravel).  Logs are chains of round cones, cut flat
(sawn) or jagged (snapped / splintered) at their ends.  Rendered with an orthographic camera by
sphere tracing, so silhouettes of straight trunks come out as straight lines (clean runs).

Buffers produced per pixel (all float/int arrays H x W):
  obj   object id (-1 = ground)          part  0 side, 1 end face (sawn / break), 2 inner wall,
  P     world position                   N     world normal
  s     arc length along the log (m)     th    angle around the axis (0 = up)
  rho   radial distance / radius on end faces
  t     ray depth
"""
import numpy as np


def norm(v):
    return v / (np.linalg.norm(v, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-12)


class Camera:
    """orthographic camera: pitch = angle below horizontal (deg), yaw = compass direction the
    camera looks toward (deg, 0 = +y), scale = px per metre, look = world point at image centre."""

    def __init__(self, W, H, scale, pitch=30.0, yaw=0.0, look=(0, 0, 0)):
        self.W, self.H, self.scale = W, H, scale
        p, y = np.radians(pitch), np.radians(yaw)
        self.f = np.array([np.cos(p) * np.sin(y), np.cos(p) * np.cos(y), -np.sin(p)])
        self.r = np.array([np.cos(y), -np.sin(y), 0.0])
        self.u = np.cross(self.r, self.f)
        self.look = np.array(look, float)
        self.pitch = pitch

    def rays(self):
        jj, ii = np.meshgrid(np.arange(self.W) + 0.5, np.arange(self.H) + 0.5)
        X = (jj - self.W / 2) / self.scale
        Y = (self.H / 2 - ii) / self.scale
        O = self.look + X[..., None] * self.r + Y[..., None] * self.u - self.f * 60.0
        D = np.broadcast_to(self.f, O.shape)
        return O, D

    def project(self, P):
        P = np.atleast_2d(P) - self.look
        x = P @ self.r * self.scale + self.W / 2
        y = self.H / 2 - P @ self.u * self.scale
        return x, y


def sd_round_cone(p, a, b, r1, r2):
    """iq's exact round-cone SDF, vectorised. p: (..., 3)."""
    ba = b - a
    l2 = ba @ ba
    rr = r1 - r2
    a2 = l2 - rr * rr
    il2 = 1.0 / l2
    pa = p - a
    y = pa @ ba
    z = y - l2
    q = pa * l2 - y[..., None] * ba
    x2 = np.einsum('...i,...i', q, q)
    y2 = y * y * l2
    z2 = z * z * l2
    k = np.sign(rr) * rr * rr * x2
    d3 = (np.sqrt(np.maximum(x2 * a2 * il2, 0)) + y * rr) * il2 - r1
    d1 = np.sqrt(x2 + z2) * il2 - r2
    d2 = np.sqrt(x2 + y2) * il2 - r1
    return np.where(np.sign(z) * a2 * z2 > k, d1, np.where(np.sign(y) * a2 * y2 < k, d2, d3))


def sd_ellipsoid(p, c, R, axes):
    """approximate ellipsoid SDF (iq's bound). axes: 3x3 rows = unit axes, R = radii."""
    q = (p - c) @ axes.T
    k0 = np.linalg.norm(q / R, axis=-1)
    k1 = np.linalg.norm(q / (R * R), axis=-1)
    return k0 * (k0 - 1.0) / (k1 + 1e-9)


def smin(a, b, k):
    h = np.clip(0.5 + 0.5 * (b - a) / k, 0, 1)
    return b * (1 - h) + a * h - k * h * (1 - h)


class Log:
    """a trunk: polyline of axis points pts (n x 3) with radii rad (n).  ends: 'sawn' | 'snapped' |
    'splinter' for the start (0) and finish (1) ends.  stubs: list of (s_frac, theta, length,
    radius_frac, tilt) branch stubs.  rootwad: None or dict(r=..., depth=..., end=0)."""

    def __init__(self, pts, rad, ends=('sawn', 'snapped'), stubs=(), rootwad=None, seed=0,
                 kind='drift', wet=0.0):
        self.pts = np.asarray(pts, float)
        self.rad = np.asarray(rad, float)
        self.ends = ends
        self.stubs = list(stubs)
        self.rootwad = rootwad
        self.seed = seed
        self.kind = kind          # 'drift' (silvered) or 'bark' (fresh)
        self.wet = wet
        seg = np.diff(self.pts, axis=0)
        self.seglen = np.linalg.norm(seg, axis=1)
        self.cum = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(self.seglen)])
        self.L = self.cum[-1]
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        # jagged-end profiles: spikes as a function of theta (a few long teeth, many short)
        self.jag = []
        for e in (0, 1):
            kind_e = ends[e]
            n = 64
            prof = np.zeros(n)
            if kind_e in ('snapped', 'splinter'):
                r_end = self.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1]
                nt = rng.integers(3, 6) if kind_e == 'snapped' else rng.integers(5, 9)
                amp = (0.6 if kind_e == 'snapped' else 2.2) * r_end
                for _ in range(nt):
                    c = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
                    w = rng.uniform(0.15, 0.5) if kind_e == 'snapped' else rng.uniform(0.08, 0.25)
                    h = amp * rng.uniform(0.3, 1.0)
                    ang = np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, n, endpoint=False)
                    dd = np.angle(np.exp(1j * (ang - c)))
                    prof = np.maximum(prof, h * np.clip(1 - np.abs(dd) / w, 0, 1))
                prof += 0.08 * r_end * rng.random(n)
            self.jag.append(prof)
        # stub primitives
        self.stub_prims = []
        for (sf, th, ln, rf, tilt) in self.stubs:
            p0, ax, r0, frame = self.frame_at(sf * self.L)
            e1, e2 = frame
            dirv = np.cos(th) * e2 + np.sin(th) * e1        # e2 ~ up
            dirv = norm(dirv + ax * tilt)
            a = p0 + dirv * r0 * 0.5
            b = p0 + dirv * (r0 + ln)
            self.stub_prims.append((a, b, r0 * rf, r0 * rf * 0.6))

    def frame_at(self, s):
        i = int(np.clip(np.searchsorted(self.cum, s) - 1, 0, len(self.seglen) - 1))
        f = (s - self.cum[i]) / self.seglen[i]
        p = self.pts[i] * (1 - f) + self.pts[i + 1] * f
        r = self.rad[i] * (1 - f) + self.rad[i + 1] * f
        ax = (self.pts[i + 1] - self.pts[i]) / self.seglen[i]
        up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
        e1 = norm(np.cross(up, ax))     # horizontal side
        e2 = np.cross(ax, e1)            # 'up' around the axis
        return p, ax, r, (e1, e2)

    def ends_frames(self):
        a0 = norm(self.pts[1] - self.pts[0])
        a1 = norm(self.pts[-1] - self.pts[-2])
        return (self.pts[0], -a0), (self.pts[-1], a1)

    def local(self, p):
        """(s, theta, rho, axis-dist) of points p relative to the polyline."""
        best = np.full(p.shape[:-1], np.inf)
        S = np.zeros(p.shape[:-1]); TH = np.zeros_like(S); RR = np.zeros_like(S)
        for i in range(len(self.seglen)):
            a, b = self.pts[i], self.pts[i + 1]
            ax = (b - a) / self.seglen[i]
            t = np.clip((p - a) @ ax, 0, self.seglen[i])
            c = a + t[..., None] * ax
            v = p - c
            d = np.linalg.norm(v, axis=-1)
            up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
            e1 = norm(np.cross(up, ax)); e2 = np.cross(ax, e1)
            th = np.arctan2(v @ e1, v @ e2)
            rloc = self.rad[i] + (self.rad[i + 1] - self.rad[i]) * t / self.seglen[i]
            m = d < best
            best = np.where(m, d, best)
            S = np.where(m, self.cum[i] + (p - a) @ ax, S)
            TH = np.where(m, th, TH)
            RR = np.where(m, d / rloc, RR)
        return S, TH, RR, best

    def sdf_body(self, p):
        d = np.full(p.shape[:-1], np.inf)
        n = len(self.pts)
        for i in range(n - 1):
            a, b = self.pts[i].copy(), self.pts[i + 1].copy()
            ax = (b - a) / self.seglen[i]
            # extend terminal segments so the end cuts make flat / jagged faces
            if i == 0:
                a = a - ax * (self.rad[0] + 0.01)
            if i == n - 2:
                b = b + ax * (self.rad[-1] + 3.0 * self.rad[-1])
            d = np.minimum(d, sd_round_cone(p, a, b, self.rad[i], self.rad[i + 1]))
        return d

    def cut(self, p, d):
        """intersect with the end half-spaces; jagged ends modulate the cut by theta."""
        parts = np.zeros(p.shape[:-1], int)
        for e, ((c, out), prof) in enumerate(zip(self.ends_frames(), self.jag)):
            up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
            e1 = norm(np.cross(up, out)); e2 = np.cross(out, e1)
            v = p - c
            th = np.arctan2(v @ e1, v @ e2)
            idx = ((th % (2 * np.pi)) / (2 * np.pi) * len(prof)).astype(int) % len(prof)
            lim = prof[idx]
            dc = v @ out - lim
            parts = np.where(dc > d, e + 1, parts)
            d = np.maximum(d, dc)
        return d, parts

    def sdf(self, p, with_parts=False):
        d = self.sdf_body(p)
        d, parts = self.cut(p, d)
        for (a, b, r1, r2) in self.stub_prims:
            ds = sd_round_cone(p, a, b, r1, r2)
            dnew = smin(d, ds, r1 * 0.6)
            parts = np.where(ds < d, 3, parts)
            d = dnew
        if self.rootwad is not None:
            rw = self.rootwad
            c, out = self.ends_frames()[rw.get('end', 0)]
            r0 = self.rad[0 if rw.get('end', 0) == 0 else -1]
            up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
            e1 = norm(np.cross(up, out)); e2 = np.cross(out, e1)
            cc = c + out * rw['depth'] * 0.5 + np.array([0, 0, rw.get('lift', 0.0)])
            axes = np.stack([out, e1, e2])
            R = np.array([rw['depth'], rw['r'], rw['r'] * rw.get('squash', 0.9)])
            de = sd_ellipsoid(p, cc, R, axes)
            parts = np.where(de < d, 4, parts)
            d = smin(d, de, r0 * 0.8)
        if with_parts:
            return d, parts
        return d

    def bound(self):
        lo = self.pts.min(0) - self.rad.max() * 4
        hi = self.pts.max(0) + self.rad.max() * 4
        for (a, b, r1, r2) in self.stub_prims:
            lo = np.minimum(lo, np.minimum(a, b) - r1); hi = np.maximum(hi, np.maximum(a, b) + r1)
        if self.rootwad is not None:
            R = self.rootwad['r'] * 1.2 + self.rootwad['depth']
            lo -= R; hi += R
        c = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        return c, np.linalg.norm(hi - lo) * 0.5


def trace(cam, logs, steps=96):
    """sphere-trace all logs; ground plane analytic.  Returns dict of buffers."""
    O, D = cam.rays()
    H, W = cam.H, cam.W
    d = cam.f
    # ground hit
    tg = -O[..., 2] / d[2]
    tbest = tg.copy()
    obj = np.full((H, W), -1)
    part = np.zeros((H, W), int)
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        c, R = lg.bound()
        # screen bbox of the bounding sphere
        sx, sy = cam.project(c)
        rp = R * cam.scale + 2
        x0, x1 = int(max(0, sx[0] - rp)), int(min(W, sx[0] + rp + 1))
        y0, y1 = int(max(0, sy[0] - rp)), int(min(H, sy[0] + rp + 1))
        if x0 >= x1 or y0 >= y1:
            continue
        o = O[y0:y1, x0:x1].reshape(-1, 3)
        # start at the bounding sphere entry
        oc = o - c
        bq = oc @ d
        cq = np.einsum('ij,ij->i', oc, oc) - R * R
        disc = bq * bq - cq
        ok = disc > 0
        t = np.where(ok, -bq - np.sqrt(np.maximum(disc, 0)), np.inf)
        tmax = np.minimum(np.where(ok, -bq + np.sqrt(np.maximum(disc, 0)), -np.inf),
                          tbest[y0:y1, x0:x1].reshape(-1))
        act = ok & (t < tmax)
        hit = np.zeros(len(o), bool)
        eps = 0.2 / cam.scale
        for _ in range(steps):
            if not act.any():
                break
            ia = np.nonzero(act)[0]
            p = o[ia] + t[ia, None] * d
            dist = lg.sdf(p)
            t[ia] += dist * 0.8
            h = dist < eps
            hit[ia[h]] = True
            act[ia[h]] = False
            far = t[ia] > tmax[ia]
            act[ia[far]] = False
        tt = np.where(hit, t, np.inf).reshape(y1 - y0, x1 - x0)
        m = tt < tbest[y0:y1, x0:x1]
        tbest[y0:y1, x0:x1] = np.where(m, tt, tbest[y0:y1, x0:x1])
        obj[y0:y1, x0:x1] = np.where(m, k, obj[y0:y1, x0:x1])
    P = O + tbest[..., None] * d
    N = np.zeros_like(P); N[..., 2] = 1.0
    s = np.zeros((H, W)); th = np.zeros((H, W)); rho = np.zeros((H, W))
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        m = obj == k
        if not m.any():
            continue
        p = P[m]
        e = 0.3 / cam.scale
        g = np.stack([lg.sdf(p + np.array([e, 0, 0])) - lg.sdf(p - np.array([e, 0, 0])),
                      lg.sdf(p + np.array([0, e, 0])) - lg.sdf(p - np.array([0, e, 0])),
                      lg.sdf(p + np.array([0, 0, e])) - lg.sdf(p - np.array([0, 0, e]))], -1)
        N[m] = norm(g)
        _, pr = lg.sdf(p, with_parts=True)
        part[m] = pr
        S, TH, RR, _ = lg.local(p)
        s[m], th[m], rho[m] = S, TH, RR
    return dict(obj=obj, part=part, P=P, N=N, s=s, th=th, rho=rho, t=tbest, cam=cam, logs=logs)


def shadow(buf, sun, logs, steps=48):
    """cast shadows: march from each surface point toward the sun against all logs."""
    P = buf['P']; H, W = P.shape[:2]
    cam = buf['cam']
    L = norm(np.asarray(sun, float))
    sh = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    p0 = (P + buf['N'] * (0.6 / cam.scale)).reshape(-1, 3)
    flat = sh.reshape(-1)
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        c, R = lg.bound()
        # candidate points: those whose sun-ray passes within R of c
        oc = p0 - c
        bq = oc @ L
        cq = np.einsum('ij,ij->i', oc, oc) - R * R
        disc = bq * bq - cq
        cand = (disc > 0) & ((-bq + np.sqrt(np.maximum(disc, 0))) > 0) & ~flat
        if not cand.any():
            continue
        ia = np.nonzero(cand)[0]
        o = p0[ia]
        t = np.maximum(0, -bq[ia] - np.sqrt(disc[ia]))
        tmax = -bq[ia] + np.sqrt(disc[ia])
        act = np.ones(len(ia), bool)
        hit = np.zeros(len(ia), bool)
        for _ in range(steps):
            if not act.any():
                break
            jb = np.nonzero(act)[0]
            dist = lg.sdf(o[jb] + t[jb, None] * L)
            t[jb] += np.maximum(dist * 0.9, 0.05 / cam.scale)
            h = dist < 0.15 / cam.scale
            hit[jb[h]] = True; act[jb[h]] = False
            act[jb[t[jb] > tmax[jb]]] = False
        flat[ia[hit]] = True
    # a surface facing away is not 'cast shadow' but form shadow; keep both separately
    return sh


def occlusion(buf, logs, radius_m):
    """cheap contact occlusion for ground points: distance to nearest log surface."""
    P = buf['P']
    dmin = np.full(P.shape[:2], np.inf)
    g = buf['obj'] == -1
    p = P[g]
    dd = np.full(len(p), np.inf)
    for lg in logs:
        dd = np.minimum(dd, lg.sdf(p))
    dmin[g] = dd
    return dmin
Write: elements/driftwood/src/paint.py
"""paint.py -- step-space painting for driftwood on gravel.

Index canvas: two arrays, mat (material id) and x (continuous step coordinate on that
material's ramp).  Marks add offsets to x; contact lines and cracks set x to hard values.
quantize() mixes only adjacent steps of one ramp with an ordered 2x2 dither.

Wood ramp (8 steps):   0 contact/crack | 1 core | 2 shadow | 3 reflected   ||   4 halftone | 5 light | 6 top | 7 end-face / glint
Gravel ramp (8 steps): same plan, keyed a step lower than wood so pale logs read against it.
"""
import numpy as np
from geo import norm

WOOD, GRAVEL, RAW, BARK, SAND = 0, 1, 2, 3, 4
BAYER2 = np.array([[0.125, 0.625], [0.875, 0.375]])
BAYER4 = np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) / 16 + 1 / 32


def value_noise(H, W, cell, rng, octaves=1):
    out = np.zeros((H, W))
    amp = 1.0; tot = 0
    for o in range(octaves):
        c = max(1, cell / (2 ** o))
        gh, gw = int(H / c) + 3, int(W / c) + 3
        g = rng.random((gh, gw))
        yy = np.arange(H) / c; xx = np.arange(W) / c
        y0 = yy.astype(int); x0 = xx.astype(int)
        fy = (yy - y0)[:, None]; fx = (xx - x0)[None, :]
        fy = fy * fy * (3 - 2 * fy); fx = fx * fx * (3 - 2 * fx)
        a = g[y0][:, x0]; b = g[y0][:, x0 + 1]; cc = g[y0 + 1][:, x0]; d = g[y0 + 1][:, x0 + 1]
        out += amp * (a * (1 - fx) * (1 - fy) + b * fx * (1 - fy) + cc * (1 - fx) * fy + d * fx * fy)
        tot += amp; amp *= 0.5
    return out / tot


def hash2(a, b, seed=0):
    """deterministic pseudo-random in [0,1) from integer arrays."""
    h = (np.asarray(a, np.int64) * 374761393 + np.asarray(b, np.int64) * 668265263 + seed * 144269504) & 0xFFFFFFFF
    h = (h ^ (h >> 13)) * 1274126177 & 0xFFFFFFFF
    h = h ^ (h >> 16)
    return (h & 0xFFFFFF) / float(0x1000000)


class Light:
    """designed light.  az: compass direction the sun comes FROM relative to the camera
    (0 = from behind the camera / front light, 90 = from the right, 180 = back light, 270 = left);
    el: elevation in degrees."""

    def __init__(self, cam, az=300.0, el=40.0, overcast=False):
        a, e = np.radians(az), np.radians(el)
        back = -cam.f * np.array([1, 1, 0]); back = back / np.linalg.norm(back)
        right = cam.r
        hor = np.cos(a) * back + np.sin(a) * right
        self.L = norm(hor * np.cos(e) + np.array([0, 0, 1.0]) * np.sin(e))
        self.overcast = overcast
        self.az, self.el = az, el


def form_wood(buf, light, sh, terminator=0.12):
    """two value families on the wood.  returns x (step coordinate) for all pixels."""
    N = buf['N']
    ndl = N @ light.L
    up = N[..., 2]
    lit = (ndl > terminator) & ~sh
    # light family 4..7 : halftone at grazing, top at full
    f = np.clip((ndl - terminator) / (0.85 - terminator), 0, 1)
    xl = 4.0 + 2.7 * f ** 0.8
    # shadow family 0.8..3.4: core just past the terminator, sky fill on up-facing, bounce
    # from the pale gravel on down-facing (the reflected light never reaches the halftone)
    sky = np.clip(up, 0, 1)
    bounce = np.clip(-up, 0, 1)
    core = np.exp(-((ndl - terminator * 0.3) / 0.22) ** 2)       # band just past terminator
    xs = 1.4 + 1.1 * sky + 1.4 * bounce ** 0.7 - 0.7 * core
    # a cast-shadowed surface that faces the sun keeps a little more (sky + it is a top)
    xs = np.where(sh & (ndl > terminator), xs + 0.4, xs)
    xs = np.clip(xs, 0.8, 3.45)
    if light.overcast:
        x = 1.6 + 3.8 * np.clip(0.5 + 0.5 * up, 0, 1) ** 1.3
        return x, np.ones_like(lit)
    return np.where(lit, xl, xs), lit


def gravel_field(buf, rng, stone_m=0.07, seed=0, spread=0.6):
    """world-space pebbles on the ground: jittered-grid cells, each a flattened dome.
    returns (inside mask, dome normal, stone value offset, cell id)."""
    P = buf['P']
    X, Y = P[..., 0], P[..., 1]
    c = stone_m
    gx, gy = np.floor(X / c).astype(int), np.floor(Y / c).astype(int)
    best = np.full(X.shape, np.inf); bnx = np.zeros_like(X); bny = np.zeros_like(X)
    brad = np.ones_like(X); bid = np.zeros(X.shape, np.int64)
    for dy in (-1, 0, 1):
        for dx in (-1, 0, 1):
            cx, cy = gx + dx, gy + dy
            jx = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 1); jy = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 2)
            rr = (0.45 + spread * hash2(cx, cy, seed + 3) ** 1.5) * c * 0.62
            ang = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 4) * np.pi
            el = 0.6 + 0.4 * hash2(cx, cy, seed + 5)
            px = (cx + 0.15 + 0.7 * jx) * c; py = (cy + 0.15 + 0.7 * jy) * c
            vx, vy = X - px, Y - py
            ca, sa = np.cos(ang), np.sin(ang)
            u = (vx * ca + vy * sa) / rr
            v = (-vx * sa + vy * ca) / (rr * el)
            d = np.sqrt(u * u + v * v)
            m = d < best
            best = np.where(m, d, best)
            bnx = np.where(m, u * ca - v * sa, bnx)
            bny = np.where(m, u * sa + v * ca, bny)
            bid = np.where(m, cx * 7919 + cy, bid)
    inside = best < 1.0
    nz = np.sqrt(np.clip(1 - best ** 2, 0, 1)) * 0.9 + 0.1
    Nst = norm(np.stack([bnx * 0.8, bny * 0.8, nz], -1))
    tone = hash2(bid, 1, seed + 9)
    return inside, Nst, tone, bid, best


def form_gravel(buf, light, sh, rng, stone_m=0.07, seed=0, occ=None):
    """ground: pebbles as small domes (light top, dark bottom), gaps dark."""
    inside, Nst, tone, bid, dist = gravel_field(buf, rng, stone_m, seed)
    ndl = Nst @ light.L
    up = Nst[..., 2]
    ground_ndl = light.L[2]
    lit = ~sh
    # stone value: its own local tone (albedo) + form
    base = 3.4 + 1.3 * (tone - 0.5)
    xl = base + 1.4 * np.clip(ndl - ground_ndl * 0.6, -1, 1)
    xs = 1.3 + 0.9 * np.clip(up, 0, 1) + 0.7 * (tone - 0.5)
    x = np.where(lit, xl, np.minimum(xs, 2.9))
    # gaps between stones: dark sand
    gap = ~inside
    x = np.where(gap, np.where(lit, 2.2, 0.9), x)
    if light.overcast:
        x = np.where(gap, 1.4, 2.6 + 1.3 * (tone - 0.5) + 1.2 * up)
    return x, inside, bid


def quantize(x, bayer=BAYER2, flat_snap=None, nsteps=8):
    """ordered dither between adjacent integer steps."""
    H, W = x.shape
    by, bx = bayer.shape
    th = np.tile(bayer, (H // by + 1, W // bx + 1))[:H, :W]
    lo = np.floor(x)
    fr = x - lo
    q = lo + (fr > th)
    return np.clip(q, 0, nsteps - 1).astype(int)


def to_rgb(mat, q, ramps):
    out = np.zeros(mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    for m, ramp in ramps.items():
        sel = mat == m
        if sel.any():
            r = np.asarray(ramp, np.uint8)
            out[sel] = r[np.clip(q[sel], 0, len(r) - 1)]
    return out


GREY_WOOD = [int(255 * v) for v in (0.05, 0.15, 0.25, 0.35, 0.56, 0.70, 0.82, 0.93)]
GREY_GRAV = [int(255 * v) for v in (0.04, 0.12, 0.21, 0.30, 0.41, 0.50, 0.58, 0.66)]
GREY_RAMPS = {WOOD: [(g, g, g) for g in GREY_WOOD], GRAVEL: [(g, g, g) for g in GREY_GRAV],
              RAW: [(g, g, g) for g in GREY_WOOD], BARK: [(max(0, g - 30),) * 3 for g in GREY_WOOD]}

Write: elements/driftwood/src/s0a_pale_trunk.py
"""Stage 0A: master copy of Ferrari's pale trunk (Living Worlds V09 'Deep Forest' morning, native
crop x 356..396, y 96..252).  The copy is painted from a procedure, not traced:

  * the copyist's sketch: the left and right silhouette columns per row (his long straight runs
    with single 1-px jogs), read from his map and then *simplified* to runs;
  * the form: a 1-D profile across the trunk -- the form classes in fixed spatial order
        rim(sky-cool) | core-dark | halftone | light | edge-falloff
    as a continuous coordinate v(u), u = across position in [0,1];
  * the quantizer: DPaint-style ordered dither between ADJACENT classes only, the dither
    matrix tiled along the axis (rows), so the seams read as 1-px checker columns.

Writes stages/s0a_*.png and prints the class-match rate on the trunk.
"""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/src'))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import re, json

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')
LW = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw')


def load(name):
    t = open(f'{LW}/{name}.json').read()
    t = re.sub(r'([{,])\s*([A-Za-z_]+)\s*:', r'\1"\2":', t).replace("'", '"')
    d = json.loads(t)
    d['pal'] = np.array(d['colors'], dtype=np.uint8)
    d['idx'] = np.array(d['pixels'], dtype=np.int32).reshape(d['height'], d['width'])
    return d


CLASSES = [132, 131, 130, 129, 128]          # rim | dark | half | light | edge, in spatial order
BAYER4 = np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) / 16 + 1 / 32


def silhouette(idx, x0, x1, y0, y1):
    m = np.isin(idx[y0:y1, x0:x1], CLASSES)
    L = np.full(y1 - y0, -1); R = np.full(y1 - y0, -1)
    for i in range(y1 - y0):
        xs = np.nonzero(m[i])[0]
        if len(xs):
            L[i], R[i] = xs.min(), xs.max()
    return L, R


def paint_trunk(L, R, breaks, mat, shift=0, h=None, w=None):
    """breaks: u positions where v crosses k+0.5 (class boundaries), len = n_classes-1."""
    out = np.full((h, w), -1)
    n = len(breaks) + 1
    for i in range(h):
        if L[i] < 0:
            continue
        xs = np.arange(L[i], R[i] + 1)
        wd = R[i] - L[i] + 1
        u = (xs - L[i] + 0.5) / wd
        # piecewise-linear v(u): v = k + 0.5 at breaks[k]
        bu = np.concatenate([[0.0], breaks, [1.0]])
        bv = np.concatenate([[0.0], np.arange(len(breaks)) + 0.5, [n - 1.0]])
        v = np.interp(u, bu, bv)
        th = mat[(i + shift) % mat.shape[0], xs % mat.shape[1]]
        lo = np.floor(v); fr = v - lo
        k = np.clip(lo + (fr > th), 0, n - 1).astype(int)
        out[i, xs] = k
    return out


def main():
    d = load('V09'); idx = d['idx']; pal = d['pal']
    x0, x1, y0, y1 = 356, 396, 118, 241
    L, R = silhouette(idx, x0, x1, y0, y1)
    ref = idx[y0:y1, x0:x1]
    refk = np.full(ref.shape, -1)
    for k, c in enumerate(CLASSES):
        refk[ref == c] = k
    tm = refk >= 0
    best = None
    mats = {'bayer4': BAYER4, 'bayer2': np.array([[0.125, 0.625], [0.875, 0.375]]),
            'vert2': np.array([[0.25, 0.75], [0.75, 0.25]]),
            'bayer4T': BAYER4.T}
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    grid = np.linspace(0.05, 0.95, 19)
    for name, mat in mats.items():
        for sh in range(mat.shape[0]):
            br = np.array([0.12, 0.40, 0.62, 0.85])
            # coordinate descent over the 4 breaks
            for it in range(4):
                for j in range(4):
                    scores = []
                    for g in grid:
                        b2 = br.copy(); b2[j] = g
                        if not np.all(np.diff(b2) > 0):
                            scores.append(-1); continue
                        cp = paint_trunk(L, R, b2, mat, sh, *ref.shape)
                        scores.append(((cp == refk) & tm).sum() / tm.sum())
                    br[j] = grid[int(np.argmax(scores))]
            cp = paint_trunk(L, R, br, mat, sh, *ref.shape)
            sc = ((cp == refk) & tm).sum() / tm.sum()
            # class-level tolerance: same class within one step
            sc1 = ((np.abs(cp - refk) <= 1) & tm).sum() / tm.sum()
            if best is None or sc > best[0]:
                best = (sc, sc1, name, sh, br.copy(), cp)
            print(f'{name} shift {sh}: exact {sc:.3f} within1 {sc1:.3f} breaks {np.round(br, 2)}')
    sc, sc1, name, sh, br, cp = best
    print('BEST', name, sh, np.round(br, 3), f'{sc:.3f}', f'{sc1:.3f}')
    # render: his background, my trunk
    out = idx[y0:y1, x0:x1].copy()
    mine = out.copy()
    mine[cp >= 0] = np.array(CLASSES)[cp[cp >= 0]]
    A = pal[out]; B = pal[mine]
    s = 8
    gap = np.full((A.shape[0], 2, 3), 255, np.uint8)
    im = np.concatenate([A, gap, B], 1)
    Image.fromarray(im).resize((im.shape[1] * s, im.shape[0] * s), Image.NEAREST).save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s0a_pale_trunk_8x.png')
    np.save(f'{ROOT}/scratch/s0a_best.npy', np.array([*br, sh]))
    return best


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

Write: elements/driftwood/src/tech.py
"""tech.py -- the driftwood techniques, each proven against a study image.

cylinder_steps  (spec: stages/s0a_*; Ferrari V09 pale trunk)
    A round trunk is five form classes in fixed spatial order across its width:
        reflected rim | core | halftone | light | lit-edge falloff
    in value steps (of an 8-step wood ramp)   3 | 2 | 4 | 6 | 5
    The shadow family starts well before the geometric terminator (Ferrari lights only ~55% of
    the visible width even with the sun 30 deg off the view axis): terminator at N.L ~ 0.7.
    The lit edge falls back one step as the form turns away; the shadow edge gets one step of
    reflected light (sky or gravel bounce) -- never reaching the halftone.
    Quantized with an ordered 2x2 dither, so each seam is a 1-px checker running ALONG the axis.
"""
import numpy as np


def cylinder_steps(N, V, L, sh=None, term=0.70, peak=0.93, edge_nv=0.42, rim_nv=0.5,
                   rim_gain=1.0, bounce_dir=None):
    """continuous step coordinate on the 8-step wood ramp.
    N: normals (...,3); V: unit vector toward the viewer; L: unit vector toward the sun;
    sh: cast-shadow mask (points in cast shadow fall into the shadow family)."""
    ndl = N @ L
    nv = np.clip(N @ V, 0, 1)
    lit = ndl > term
    if sh is not None:
        lit &= ~sh
    # light family: halftone (4) at the terminator rising to light (6) at the peak
    f = np.clip((ndl - term) / (peak - term), 0, 1)
    xl = 3.6 + 2.8 * f
    # lit-edge falloff: where the lit surface turns away from the viewer, one step back
    xl = np.where(nv < edge_nv, np.minimum(xl, 5.4), xl)
    # shadow family: core (2) inside, reflected rim (3) where the form turns away (low N.V)
    rim = np.clip((rim_nv - nv) / rim_nv, 0, 1)
    if bounce_dir is not None:
        rim = rim * np.clip(0.3 + 0.7 * (N @ bounce_dir), 0, 1)
    xs = 1.6 + 1.9 * rim * rim_gain
    xs = np.minimum(xs, 3.45)
    return np.where(lit, xl, xs), lit
Write: elements/driftwood/src/s0a2_pipeline_copy.py
"""Stage 0A-2: the same pale trunk re-painted by the general pipeline (3-D cylinder, designed
light, cylinder_steps, ordered dither), with his palette, and compared to his crop.
This proves the technique is geometry-driven (so it can be rotated to lie down) and still
matches the master."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from s0a_pale_trunk import load, silhouette, CLASSES, ROOT
import geo, tech
from paint import quantize, BAYER2

d = load('V09'); idx = d['idx']; pal = d['pal']
x0, x1, y0, y1 = 356, 396, 126, 241
ref = idx[y0:y1, x0:x1]
L_, R_ = silhouette(idx, x0, x1, y0, y1)
H, W = ref.shape
# his value-ordered ramp mapped onto wood steps 2..6
STEP2IDX = {2: 131, 3: 132, 4: 130, 5: 128, 6: 129}


def paint(az_deg=38.0, term=0.70, peak=0.93, edge_nv=0.42, rim_nv=0.5, phase=(0, 0)):
    # per row: a cylinder cross-section fitted to his silhouette (the copyist's sketch)
    out = np.full((H, W), -1)
    V = np.array([0, -1.0, 0])       # toward viewer (camera looks +y)
    a = np.radians(az_deg)
    Lv = np.array([np.sin(a), -np.cos(a), 0.25]); Lv /= np.linalg.norm(Lv)
    X = np.zeros((H, W))
    M = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    for i in range(H):
        if L_[i] < 0:
            continue
        xs = np.arange(L_[i], R_[i] + 1)
        c = 0.5 * (L_[i] + R_[i] + 1); r = 0.5 * (R_[i] - L_[i] + 1)
        sx = np.clip((xs + 0.5 - c) / r, -0.999, 0.999)
        N = np.stack([sx, -np.sqrt(1 - sx ** 2), np.zeros_like(sx)], -1)
        x, _ = tech.cylinder_steps(N, V, Lv, term=term, peak=peak, edge_nv=edge_nv, rim_nv=rim_nv)
        X[i, xs] = x; M[i, xs] = True
    Xp = np.roll(np.roll(X, phase[0], 0), phase[1], 1)
    q = quantize(X if phase == (0, 0) else X, BAYER2, nsteps=8)
    if phase != (0, 0):
        th = np.tile(BAYER2, (H // 2 + 2, W // 2 + 2))[phase[0]:phase[0] + H, phase[1]:phase[1] + W]
        q = np.clip(np.floor(X) + ((X - np.floor(X)) > th), 0, 7).astype(int)
    q = np.clip(q, 2, 6)
    out[M] = np.vectorize(STEP2IDX.get)(q[M])
    return out, M


def score(out, M):
    return (out[M] == ref[M]).mean()


best = None
for az in (20, 30, 38, 45, 55):
    for term in (0.55, 0.62, 0.70, 0.78):
        for edge in (0.3, 0.42, 0.55):
            for rim in (0.35, 0.5, 0.65):
                for ph in ((0, 0), (1, 0), (0, 1), (1, 1)):
                    o, M = paint(az, term, 0.93, edge, rim, ph)
                    s = score(o, M)
                    if best is None or s > best[0]:
                        best = (s, az, term, edge, rim, ph)

    print(name, ' '.join(cols))

Write: elements/driftwood/src/tech.py
"""tech.py -- the driftwood techniques, each proven against a study image.

cylinder_steps  (spec: stages/s0a2_*; Ferrari V09 pale trunk)
    A round trunk is five form classes in fixed spatial order across its width:
        reflected rim | core | halftone | light | lit-edge falloff
    in value steps (of an 8-step wood ramp)   3 | 2 | 4 | 6 | 5
    Explicit thresholds, not a lighting formula: the shadow family starts well before the
    geometric terminator (Ferrari lights only ~55% of the visible width with the sun ~30 deg off
    the view axis), the light class is a narrow band at the peak, the lit edge falls back one
    step as the form turns away, and the shadow edge gets one step of reflected light (sky or
    gravel bounce) -- never reaching the halftone.
    Each boundary is a short linear ramp, so the ordered 2x2 quantizer turns it into a 1-px
    checker seam running ALONG the axis.
"""
import numpy as np

CYL = dict(term=0.74, w_term=0.10, peak=0.965, w_peak=0.03, edge_nv=0.70, w_edge=0.12,
           rim_nv=0.62, w_rim=0.15)


def ramp(v, at, w):
    return np.clip((v - at) / w + 0.5, 0, 1)


def cylinder_steps(N, V, L, sh=None, p=None, bounce_dir=None):
    """continuous step coordinate on the 8-step wood ramp (+ lit mask)."""
    q = dict(CYL); q.update(p or {})
    ndl = N @ L
    nv = np.clip(N @ V, 0, 1)
    t = ramp(ndl, q['term'], q['w_term'])
    if sh is not None:
        t = np.where(sh, 0.0, t)
    x = 2.0 + 2.0 * t + 2.0 * ramp(ndl, q['peak'], q['w_peak']) * (t > 0.99)
    # lit-edge falloff (light family only)
    e = ramp(q['edge_nv'] - nv, 0, q['w_edge'])
    x = np.where(x > 5.0, x - e * (x - 5.0), x)
    # reflected rim (shadow family only); a bounce direction can weight it (gravel below)
    r = ramp(q['rim_nv'] - nv, 0, q['w_rim'])
    if bounce_dir is not None:
        r = r * np.clip(0.35 + 0.65 * (N @ bounce_dir), 0, 1)
    x = np.where(t < 0.5, np.minimum(x + r * 1.0, 3.45), x)
    return x, t >= 0.5

Write: elements/driftwood/src/tech.py
"""tech.py -- the driftwood techniques, each proven against a study image.

cylinder_classes  (spec: stages/s0a2_*; Ferrari V09 pale trunk)
    A round trunk is five FORM CLASSES in fixed spatial order across its width, counted from
    the shadow edge toward the lit edge:
        0 reflected rim | 1 core | 2 halftone | 3 light | 4 lit-edge falloff
    painted on the wood ramp at steps           3 | 2 | 4 | 6 | 5
    The class coordinate is a smooth function of the angle d around the axis, measured from the
    light's peak (d < 0 toward the shadow edge), with class boundaries (Ferrari, measured):
        rim | -83 deg | core | -42 | halftone | -13 | light | +14 | edge
    So the core starts ~7 deg BEFORE the geometric terminator, the halftone is a 30 deg band,
    the light only ~27 deg wide at the peak.  No pure zones: the coordinate runs linearly
    through every class and the quantizer mixes ONLY spatially adjacent classes with a 3-level
    checker (pure / 50% / pure), so every seam is a 1-2 px checker running along the axis.
    Dithering follows class adjacency, not value adjacency: core (step 2) dithers straight into
    halftone (step 4), skipping the cool rim colour (step 3).
"""
import numpy as np

CHECK3 = np.array([[0.25, 0.75], [0.75, 0.25]])     # 3-level checker (Ferrari's trunk seams)
CLASS_STEPS = np.array([3, 2, 4, 6, 5])
CYL_BREAKS = np.array([-83.0, -42.0, -13.0, 14.0])  # degrees from the light peak


def normal_plane_angles(N, V, L, T):
    """angle of N and of the light around the local axis T, both measured in the plane
    perpendicular to T from the view direction; returns d = signed angle from the light peak,
    oriented so that +d points toward the lit silhouette edge."""
    def proj(a):
        a = a - (a * T).sum(-1, keepdims=True) * T
        return a / (np.linalg.norm(a, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9)
    Vp = proj(np.broadcast_to(V, N.shape))
    B = np.cross(T, Vp)
    Lp = proj(np.broadcast_to(L, N.shape))
    phN = np.degrees(np.arctan2((N * B).sum(-1), (N * Vp).sum(-1)))
    phL = np.degrees(np.arctan2((Lp * B).sum(-1), (Lp * Vp).sum(-1)))
    sgn = np.where(phL >= 0, 1.0, -1.0)
    d = (phN - phL) * sgn
    d = (d + 180) % 360 - 180
    return d, phL


def cylinder_classes(d, breaks=CYL_BREAKS, sh=None):
    """continuous class coordinate v in [0, 4] from the peak angle d."""
    bu = np.concatenate([[-180.0], breaks, [180.0]])
    bv = np.concatenate([[-0.5], np.arange(len(breaks)) + 0.5, [len(breaks) + 0.5]])
    # linear between break midpoints: v = k at the middle of class k
    mids = np.concatenate([[breaks[0] - 25], 0.5 * (breaks[1:] + breaks[:-1]), [breaks[-1] + 30]])
    v = np.interp(d, np.concatenate([[-180], mids, [180]]),
                  np.concatenate([[0], np.arange(len(mids)), [len(mids) - 1]]))
    if sh is not None:
        v = np.where(sh, np.minimum(v, 1.0), v)
    return np.clip(v, 0, len(breaks))


def quantize_classes(v, mat=CHECK3, phase=(0, 0), class_steps=CLASS_STEPS):
    H, W = v.shape
    th = np.tile(mat, (H // mat.shape[0] + 2, W // mat.shape[1] + 2))
    th = th[phase[0]:phase[0] + H, phase[1]:phase[1] + W]
    lo = np.floor(v)
    k = np.clip(lo + ((v - lo) > th), 0, len(class_steps) - 1).astype(int)
    return class_steps[k], k
Bash: Refit with class-coordinate cylinder model
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('tech.py').read()
a=s.index('def cylinder_classes'); b=s.index('def quantize_classes')
s=s[:a]+'''def cylinder_classes(d, phL, breaks=CYL_BREAKS, sh=None):
    """continuous class coordinate v in [0, 4]: piecewise linear through the breaks (v = k+0.5
    at break k), from v = 0 at the shadow silhouette to v = 4 at the lit silhouette."""
    aL = np.abs(phL)
    d0 = np.minimum(-90.0 - aL, breaks[0] - 1)
    d1 = np.maximum(90.0 - aL, breaks[-1] + 1)
    n = len(breaks)
    v = np.zeros_like(d)
    # segment 0: [d0, b0] -> [0, 0.5]; middle segments; last: [b_last, d1] -> [n-0.5, n]
    xs = [d0] + [np.full_like(d, b) for b in breaks] + [d1]
    ys = [0.0] + [k + 0.5 for k in range(n)] + [float(n)]
    for k in range(len(xs) - 1):
        lo, hi = xs[k], xs[k + 1]
        m = (d >= lo) & (d < hi)
        f = (d - lo) / np.maximum(hi - lo, 1e-6)
        v = np.where(m, ys[k] + f * (ys[k + 1] - ys[k]), v)
    v = np.where(d >= d1, n, v)
    v = np.where(d < d0, 0.0, v)
    if sh is not None:
        v = np.where(sh, np.minimum(v, 1.0), v)
    return np.clip(v, 0, n)

"""util.py -- labelled side-by-side boards at integer zoom."""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont


def font(sz=14):
    for p in ('/System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc', '/System/Library/Fonts/Monaco.ttf'):
        try:
            return ImageFont.truetype(p, sz)
        except Exception:
            pass
    return ImageFont.load_default()


def zoom(a, s):
    a = np.asarray(a)
    return np.repeat(np.repeat(a, s, 0), s, 1)


def board(panels, scale=8, pad=6, label_h=20, bg=(24, 24, 28), cols=None, title=None, fsz=13):
    """panels: list of (label, HxWx3 uint8).  Each panel zoomed by scale (or its own scale if a
    3-tuple (label, img, s))."""
    ims = []
    for p in panels:
        lab, im = p[0], np.asarray(p[1])
        s = p[2] if len(p) > 2 else scale
        if im.ndim == 2:
            im = np.stack([im] * 3, -1)
        ims.append((lab, zoom(im.astype(np.uint8), s)))
    cols = cols or len(ims)
    rows = (len(ims) + cols - 1) // cols
    cw = max(i.shape[1] for _, i in ims); ch = max(i.shape[0] for _, i in ims)
    th = 26 if title else 0
    W = cols * (cw + pad) + pad; H = th + rows * (ch + label_h + pad) + pad
    out = Image.new('RGB', (W, H), bg)
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(out); f = font(fsz)
    if title:
        d.text((pad, 4), title, fill=(230, 230, 230), font=font(fsz + 2))
    for n, (lab, im) in enumerate(ims):
        r, c = divmod(n, cols)
        x = pad + c * (cw + pad); y = th + pad + r * (ch + label_h + pad)
        d.text((x, y + 2), lab, fill=(210, 210, 210), font=f)
        out.paste(Image.fromarray(im), (x, y + label_h))
    return out


def fit_photo(path, box, size):
    """crop a photo box (x0,y0,x1,y1) and resize to size (w,h) with area filtering."""
    im = Image.open(path).convert('RGB').crop(box)
    return np.array(im.resize(size, Image.LANCZOS))

from s0a_pale_trunk import load
import numpy as np
d=load('V02'); idx=d['idx']

# rim rule candidates: exposed toward direction (dy,dx)
def exposed(M,dy,dx):
  s=np.zeros_like(M); P=np.pad(M,1)
  return M & ~P[1+dy:1+dy+M.shape[0],1+dx:1+dx+M.shape[1]]
for name,e in [('right',exposed(M,0,1)),('up',exposed(M,-1,0)),('upright',exposed(M,-1,1)),('right|up',exposed(M,0,1)|exposed(M,-1,0)),('right|upright',exposed(M,0,1)|exposed(M,-1,1))]:
  tp=(e&rim).sum(); print(name,'precision %.2f recall %.2f'%(tp/max(1,e.sum()), tp/rim.sum()))

from collections import Counter
c=Counter()
for i in range(M.shape[0]):
  run=0
  for j in range(M.shape[1]):
    if rim[i,j]: run+=1
    else:
      if run: c[run]+=1
      run=0
print('horizontal rim run lengths',sorted(c.items()))

Write: elements/driftwood/src/render.py
"""render.py -- driftwood on gravel: world -> index canvas (mat, step) -> pixels.

Layers (painter core):
  1 form     geo.trace + tech.cylinder_classes (wood), pebble domes (gravel)
  2 value    families: cast shadow forces the shadow family; contact line at 0
  3 marks    (stage 2+) grain, cracks, knots
  4 drawing  contact line, bottom row of the log, silhouette runs
  5 quantize class checker (wood), ordered 2x2 (gravel)
  6 palette  (stage 4) ramps per material
"""
import numpy as np
import geo, tech
from paint import (WOOD, GRAVEL, RAW, BARK, Light, gravel_field, hash2, BAYER2)

GREY = {
    WOOD:   [22, 48, 84, 108, 140, 172, 202, 232],
    GRAVEL: [18, 40, 64, 86, 108, 128, 146, 164],
    RAW:    [22, 48, 84, 108, 150, 182, 212, 240],
    BARK:   [14, 30, 50, 66, 84, 102, 120, 140],
}


def grey_ramps():
    return {m: [(g, g, g) for g in v] for m, v in GREY.items()}


class Canvas:
    def __init__(self, H, W):
        self.mat = np.full((H, W), GRAVEL)
        self.step = np.zeros((H, W), int)


def render(logs, cam, light, seed=0, stone_m=0.06, opts=None):
    o = dict(contact=True, bottom_row=True, gravel=True, cast=True)
    o.update(opts or {})
    H, W = cam.H, cam.W
    buf = geo.trace(cam, logs)
    sh = geo.shadow(buf, light.L, logs) if o['cast'] and not light.overcast else np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    cv = Canvas(H, W)
    V = -cam.f
    obj = buf['obj']
    wood = obj >= 0
    # ---------- wood form: five classes around the axis
    d, phL = tech.normal_plane_angles(buf['N'], V, light.L, buf['T'])
    v = tech.cylinder_classes(d, phL, sh=sh)
    if light.overcast:
        # overcast: the sky is the light; peak straight up
        d2, ph2 = tech.normal_plane_angles(buf['N'], V, np.array([0, 0, 1.0]), buf['T'])
        v = tech.cylinder_classes(d2, ph2)
    st, k = tech.quantize_classes(v)
    part = buf['part']
    # end faces (sawn): a flat plane -- one class for the whole face, by its facing
    endf = wood & np.isin(part, (1, 2))
    ndl = (buf['N'] * light.L).sum(-1)
    face_step = np.where((ndl > 0.35) & ~sh, 7, np.where(ndl > 0.0, 4, 3))
    if light.overcast:
        face_step = np.full_like(face_step, 5)
    st = np.where(endf, face_step, st)
    cv.mat[wood] = WOOD
    cv.step[wood] = st[wood]
    # ---------- gravel form
    g = ~wood
    if o['gravel']:
        gx = gravel_steps(buf, light, sh, stone_m, seed, cam)
        cv.step[g] = gx[g]
    else:
        cv.step[g] = np.where(sh, 2, 4)[g]
    # ---------- drawing: contact line and the log's bottom row
    if o['contact']:
        contact(cv, buf, logs, cam)
    return cv, buf, sh


def gravel_steps(buf, light, sh, stone_m, seed, cam):
    """pebbles: each a flattened dome.  Painted with Ferrari's stone rule: the top of each stone
    light, its bottom row dark, the gaps darkest; cast shadow drops the whole stone into the
    shadow family (steps 1-3) but keeps a lit top pixel one step up."""
    inside, Nst, tone, bid, dist = gravel_field(buf, None, stone_m, seed)
    L = light.L
    ndl = (Nst * L).sum(-1)
    up = Nst[..., 2]
    base = 4.0 + 1.6 * (tone - 0.5)
    x_l = base + 1.6 * (ndl - L[2])
    x_s = 1.8 + 0.8 * up + 0.8 * (tone - 0.5)
    x = np.where(sh, np.minimum(x_s, 3.3), x_l)
    gap = ~inside
    x = np.where(gap, np.where(sh, 0.6, 1.8), x)
    if light.overcast:
        x = np.where(gap, 1.2, 3.0 + 1.4 * (tone - 0.5) + 1.2 * (up - 0.8))
    H, W = x.shape
    th = np.tile(BAYER2, (H // 2 + 1, W // 2 + 1))[:H, :W]
    q = np.floor(x) + ((x - np.floor(x)) > th)
    return np.clip(q, 0, 7).astype(int)


def contact(cv, buf, logs, cam):
    """1 px darkest line where a log meets the ground: ground pixels directly below the log's
    silhouette whose world point lies within ~1.5 px of the log surface; the log's own bottom
    row one class darker (the form turning under)."""
    obj = buf['obj']
    wood = obj >= 0
    below = np.zeros_like(wood); below[1:] = wood[:-1] & ~wood[1:]
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(below)
    P = buf['P']
    for y, x in zip(ys, xs):
        lg = logs[obj[y - 1, x]]
        dd = lg.sdf(P[y, x][None])[0]
        if dd < 2.0 / cam.scale:
            cv.step[y, x] = 0
            cv.mat[y, x] = GRAVEL
    # bottom row of the log where it touches: darkest wood class
    bot = wood.copy(); bot[:-1] &= ~wood[1:]
    by, bx = np.nonzero(bot)
    for y, x in zip(by, bx):
        if y + 1 < wood.shape[0] and cv.step[y + 1, x] == 0 and cv.mat[y + 1, x] == GRAVEL:
            cv.step[y, x] = min(cv.step[y, x], 1)


def to_rgb(cv, ramps):
    out = np.zeros(cv.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    for m, r in ramps.items():
        sel = cv.mat == m
        out[sel] = np.asarray(r, np.uint8)[np.clip(cv.step[sel], 0, len(r) - 1)]
    return out
Write: elements/driftwood/src/s1_log.py
"""Stage 1: one log in greyscale, lying on gravel, lit from several directions."""
import sys, os, time
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')


def one_log(L=3.0, r=0.14, yaw_log=15.0, ends=('sawn', 'sawn'), seed=1, taper=0.8, sink=0.02):
    a = np.radians(yaw_log)
    ax = np.array([np.cos(a), np.sin(a), 0.0])
    p0 = -ax * L / 2; p1 = ax * L / 2
    z0 = r - sink; z1 = r * taper - sink
    pts = [p0 + [0, 0, z0], p1 + [0, 0, z1]]
    return geo.Log(pts, [r, r * taper], ends=ends, seed=seed)


def cell(light_az, light_el, pitch=30.0, scale=40.0, W=160, H=80, **kw):
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, yaw=0.0)
    lg = one_log(**kw)
    li = Light(cam, az=light_az, el=light_el, overcast=(light_az is None))
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, li, seed=3)
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())


if __name__ == '__main__':
    t = time.time()
    lights = [('left 40', 270, 40), ('right 40', 90, 40), ('front 40', 0, 40), ('back 25', 180, 25),
              ('top 70', 300, 70), ('overcast', None, 0)]
    panels = [(n, cell(a, e)) for n, a, e in lights]
    board(panels, scale=4, cols=2, title='s1 one log, greyscale, 30 deg oblique, 40 px/m').save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s1_lights_4x.png')
    print('done', time.time() - t)

# output:
"""tech_ground.py -- techniques for granular ground (cinder, soil, gravel far off).

Each function is one technique: a trigger (documented), a recipe, and parameters that come from
measurement (photo statistic x Ferrari's ratio for the nearest class he painted).

  grain(ctx, mask, mat, cluster_px, amp)       clumped stipple in step space: 'texture without strokes'
  fall_streaks(ctx, mask, mat, ...)            1-px downslope lines (ravel streaks on cinder, runnels
                                               on a scarp): narrow forms -> clean runs
  specks(ctx, mask, mat, density, ...)         lone light clasts, each a light-top / dark-bottom pair
  clumped_threshold(h, w, rng, sigma)          noise with a target cluster size, histogram-flattened
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from noise import fbm, value

CLEAN = np.array([0, 0.25, 1 / 3, 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 99])


def clumped(h, w, rng, sigma, aniso=(1.0, 1.0), angle=0.0):
    """uniform-[0,1) field whose blobs have ~ (2*sigma)^2 px area; optionally stretched along
    `angle` (degrees, screen, 0 = horizontal) by aniso=(along, across)."""
    n = rng.random((h + 8, w + 8))
    if aniso != (1.0, 1.0):
        n = ndi.rotate(n, -angle, reshape=False, mode="wrap", order=1)
        n = ndi.gaussian_filter(n, (sigma * aniso[1], sigma * aniso[0]), mode="wrap")
        n = ndi.rotate(n, angle, reshape=False, mode="wrap", order=1)
    else:
        n = ndi.gaussian_filter(n, sigma, mode="wrap")
    n = n[4:-4, 4:-4]
    r = np.argsort(np.argsort(n.ravel())).reshape(h, w)
    return (r + 0.5) / r.size


# measured by studies/grain_cal.py: gaussian sigma -> mean cluster area (px) of a 4-level cut
_CAL_S = np.array([0.0, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0])
_CAL_A = np.array([1.93, 2.04, 3.46, 5.29, 10.17, 20.48, 96.8])


def sigma_for_cluster(px_area):
    """invert the calibration: the sigma that gives a mean cluster area of px_area."""
    return float(np.interp(px_area, _CAL_A, _CAL_S))


def grain(ctx, mask, mat, cluster_px, amp=0.9, aniso=(1.0, 1.0), angle=0.0, bias=0.0):
    """trigger: any granular surface too far for single grains (grain < ~2 px).
    recipe: offset the form coordinate by clumped noise of the TARGET cluster size (photo cluster x
    Ferrari's cluster ratio, ~2x for granular ground); the mass value stays put, the texture lives
    inside it."""
    C = ctx.C
    s = sigma_for_cluster(cluster_px)
    t = clumped(C.h, C.w, ctx.rng, max(s, 0.01), aniso, angle) if s > 0.05 else ctx.rng.random((C.h, C.w))
    sel = mask & (C.mat == mat) & ~C.hard
    C.x[sel] += amp * (t[sel] - 0.5) * 2 + bias


def _clean_step(dx, dy):
    """snap a screen direction to a clean pixel-art slope; returns (sx, sy) per-step increments."""
    if abs(dx) < 1e-6 and abs(dy) < 1e-6:
        return 0, 1
    if abs(dy) >= abs(dx):

"""gravel.py -- the gravel bar under the logs, painted as stamped pebble glyphs (not per-pixel
shading, which at 2-3 px per stone is TV noise).

Trigger: the ground around driftwood.  Recipe (Ferrari's stone rule + 'contrast inside the mark'):

  * a calm base: the ground mass sits on ONE step (lit: 4, shadow: 2), with a faint clumped
    two-step mottle for the albedo variety of mixed river stones;
  * pebbles are glyphs chosen by their size in pixels:
        < 1.3 px   a lone speck, light or dark by albedo
        1.3 - 2.6  a pair: light pixel over a dark pixel (the smallest light-top/dark-bottom mark)
        2.6 - 5    a lozenge: lit top row, body at its albedo, dark bottom row, flat ends
        > 5        a cobble: dome classes (lit cap, halftone, core on the far side), flat dark
                   contact row beneath
    the lit side follows the sun's screen direction; in cast shadow a glyph drops into the
    shadow family (its light pixel becomes step 3, never reaching the lit base);
  * coverage is sparse near the viewer-facing flat and denser with distance (foreshortening),
    so the mass stays calm and the marks carry the texture.
"""
import numpy as np
from paint import hash2, value_noise


def paint_gravel(buf, cam, light, sh, seed=0, stone_m=0.05, density=0.55, mottle=0.6,
                 size_sigma=0.45):
    H, W = cam.H, cam.W
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    P = buf['P']
    ground = buf['obj'] == -1
    lit_base, sh_base = 4.0, 2.0
    x = np.where(sh, sh_base, lit_base).astype(float)
    # albedo mottle in world space (clumps of lighter / darker stones), 2 steps at most
    X, Y = P[..., 0], P[..., 1]
    cell = max(stone_m * 3.0, 3.0 / cam.scale)
    mot = _world_noise(X / cell, Y / cell, seed)
    x += mottle * (mot - 0.5) * 1.6
    xq = _checker(x)
    # stamp pebbles, far to near
    gx = X[ground]; gy = Y[ground]
    if len(gx) == 0:
        return xq
    x0, x1, y0, y1 = gx.min(), gx.max(), gy.min(), gy.max()
    area = (x1 - x0) * (y1 - y0)
    n = int(area / (stone_m * stone_m) * density)
    n = min(n, 60000)
    px = rng.uniform(x0, x1, n); py = rng.uniform(y0, y1, n)
    size = stone_m * np.exp(rng.normal(0, size_sigma, n))
    alb = rng.random(n)
    pts = np.stack([px, py, np.zeros(n)], 1)
    sx, sy = cam.project(pts)
    order = np.argsort(sy)      # far (top) first
    Ls = light.L
    # screen direction of the light (for the lit side of each glyph)
    lx = Ls @ cam.r; ly = -(Ls @ cam.u)
    step = xq.copy()
    s_pitch = np.sin(np.radians(cam.pitch))
    for i in order:
        cx, cy = sx[i], sy[i]
        ix, iy = int(np.floor(cx)), int(np.floor(cy))
        if ix < 0 or ix >= W or iy < 0 or iy >= H or not ground[iy, ix]:
            continue
        spx = size[i] * cam.scale
        shd = sh[iy, ix]
        a = alb[i]
        body = (4 if a > 0.3 else 3) if not shd else 2
        if a > 0.85 and not shd:
            body = 5
        hi = min(body + 1, 6) if not shd else 3
        lo = body - 2 if not shd else 1
        if spx < 1.3:
            if hash2(i, 7, seed) < 0.5:
                _put(step, ground, iy, ix, hi if a > 0.5 else lo)
        elif spx < 2.6:
            # pair: light over dark (sun from above); for side light the pair turns sideways
            if abs(lx) > 0.8 and abs(ly) < 0.4:
                dx = 1 if lx > 0 else -1
                _put(step, ground, iy, ix + dx, hi); _put(step, ground, iy, ix, lo)
            else:
                up = -1 if ly <= 0.2 else 1
                _put(step, ground, iy + up, ix, hi if up == -1 else lo)
                _put(step, ground, iy, ix, lo if up == -1 else hi)
        elif spx < 5.0:
            w = int(round(spx)); h = max(2, int(round(spx * max(s_pitch, 0.35))))
            _lozenge(step, ground, ix - w // 2, iy - h + 1, w, h, body, hi, lo, lx, ly, shd)
        else:
            w = int(round(spx)); h = max(3, int(round(spx * max(s_pitch, 0.35) + spx * 0.35)))
            _cobble(step, ground, cx, cy, w, h, body, lx, ly, shd)
    return step


def _put(step, ground, y, x, v):
    H, W = step.shape
    if 0 <= y < H and 0 <= x < W and ground[y, x]:
        step[y, x] = v


def _lozenge(step, ground, x0, y0, w, h, body, hi, lo, lx, ly, shd):
    """a small stone: flat ends cut at 45 deg (the corner pixels dropped), lit top row, dark
    bottom row, and a darkest contact pixel row under its middle."""
    for r in range(h):
        inset = 1 if (r == 0 or r == h - 1) and w > 2 else 0
        for c in range(inset, w - inset):
            v = body
            if r == 0:
                v = hi
            elif r == h - 1:
                v = lo
            # side light: the sun side column one step up, the far column one down
            if abs(lx) > 0.6 and w >= 3:
                if (c == w - 1 - inset and lx > 0) or (c == inset and lx < 0):
                    v = max(v, hi) if r < h - 1 else v
                if (c == inset and lx > 0) or (c == w - 1 - inset and lx < 0):
                    v = min(v, body - 1)
            _put(step, ground, y0 + r, x0 + c, v)
    for c in range(1, w - 1):
        _put(step, ground, y0 + h, x0 + c, max(0, lo - 1))


def _cobble(step, ground, cx, cy, w, h, body, lx, ly, shd):
    H, W = step.shape
    x0 = int(cx - w / 2); y0 = int(cy - h + 1)
    for r in range(h):
        for c in range(w):
            u = (c + 0.5) / w * 2 - 1
            v = (r + 0.5) / h * 2 - 1
            # flat-bottomed dome: ellipse on top, flat run at the bottom
            if v < 0.4 and u * u + ((v - 0.4) / 1.4) ** 2 > 1:
                continue
            if v >= 0.4 and abs(u) > 1 - (v - 0.4) * 0.5:
                continue
            nz = max(0.0, 1 - u * u - max(0, (v - 0.4) / 1.4) ** 2)
            ndl = (u * lx * 0.8 + (-v) * (-ly) * 0.8 + nz) if not shd else -1
            val = body
            if ndl > 1.0:
                val = body + 1
            elif ndl < 0.35:
                val = body - 1
            if r == h - 1:
                val = body - 2
            _put(step, ground, y0 + r, x0 + c, val)
    for c in range(1, w - 1):
        _put(step, ground, y0 + h, x0 + c, max(0, body - 3))


def _world_noise(u, v, seed):
    iu, iv = np.floor(u).astype(np.int64), np.floor(v).astype(np.int64)
    fu, fv = u - iu, v - iv
    fu = fu * fu * (3 - 2 * fu); fv = fv * fv * (3 - 2 * fv)
    a = hash2(iu, iv, seed); b = hash2(iu + 1, iv, seed)
    c = hash2(iu, iv + 1, seed); d = hash2(iu + 1, iv + 1, seed)
    return a * (1 - fu) * (1 - fv) + b * fu * (1 - fv) + c * (1 - fu) * fv + d * fu * fv


def _checker(x):
    H, W = x.shape
    th = np.tile(np.array([[0.25, 0.75], [0.75, 0.25]]), (H // 2 + 1, W // 2 + 1))[:H, :W]
    return np.clip(np.floor(x) + ((x - np.floor(x)) > th), 0, 7).astype(int)

"""Stage 1 comparison: photo crop | my log | Ferrari trunk (rotated to lie down), at 8x."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board, fit_photo
from s0a_pale_trunk import load

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')
PHOTO = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg')


def hero(W=72, H=56, scale=26.0, az=235, el=45, pitch=22, seed=3, yaw_log=35, r=0.24, L=3.2, ends=('sawn', 'snapped')):
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, yaw=0.0, look=(0.1, 0, 0.1))
    a = np.radians(yaw_log)
    ax = np.array([np.cos(a), np.sin(a), 0.0])
    lg = geo.Log([-ax * L / 2 + [0, 0, r - 0.03], ax * L / 2 + [0, 0, r * 0.85 - 0.03]], [r, r * 0.85],
                 ends=ends, seed=seed)
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el)
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, li, seed=seed, stone_m=0.05)
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())


if __name__ == '__main__':
    ph = fit_photo(PHOTO, (310, 470, 526, 638), (72, 56))
    phg = np.repeat(np.array(Image.fromarray(ph).convert('L'))[..., None], 3, -1)
    mine = hero()
    d = load('V09'); f = d['pal'][d['idx'][150:222, 358:414]]
    f = np.rot90(f, 1)            # his trunk, laid down
    board([('photo (grey, /3)', phg), ('mine', mine), ('Ferrari V09 trunk, laid down', f)], scale=8,
          title='s1 one log: photo | pixels | Ferrari').save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s1_compare_8x.png')

"""cobble.py -- one water-worn cobble, painted in step space.

Stage 1 technique: the geometry is a lumpy flattened ellipsoid sitting (slightly sunk) on a
gravel plane, seen by an orthographic camera pitched down.  Everything is decided per pixel
as a STEP on one grey ramp (the palette is the light, added later):

    0 contact / crevice      (black pocket where stone meets ground)
    1 core shadow            (the band just past the terminator: gets neither sun nor bounce)
    2 shadow                 (body of the shadow side, lit by sky)
    3 reflected light        (lower shadow side, lit by bounce from sunlit gravel)
   ---------- terminator: the two families never mix -------------
    4 halftone               (surface turning away from the sun)
    5 light                  (plane facing the sun)
    6 top light              (small, only where the normal is closest to the sun)

Ground: 4 lit gravel, 2 cast shadow, 0 contact.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi

BAYER2 = np.array([[0, 2], [3, 1]]) / 4.0 + 1 / 8.0
BAYER4 = (np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) + 0.5) / 16.0

GREY7 = [(22, 22, 26), (52, 52, 58), (80, 80, 86), (112, 112, 116), (146, 146, 148), (184, 184, 184), (222, 222, 218)]


def unit(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, float)
    return v / np.linalg.norm(v)


def sun_dir(azimuth_deg, elev_deg):
    """azimuth measured in the ground plane from screen-right (+x) toward away-from-viewer (+y).
    135 = sun upper-left-behind the stone (classic painter's light)."""
    a, e = np.radians(azimuth_deg), np.radians(elev_deg)
    return np.array([np.cos(a) * np.cos(e), np.sin(a) * np.cos(e), np.sin(e)])


class View:
    def __init__(self, pitch_deg=35.0):
        p = np.radians(pitch_deg)
        self.d = np.array([0.0, np.cos(p), -np.sin(p)])   # view direction (into screen)
        self.u = np.array([0.0, np.sin(p), np.cos(p)])    # screen up
        self.r = np.array([1.0, 0.0, 0.0])

    def project(self, P):
        """world points (...,3) -> screen x, screen y (down), depth (smaller = nearer)."""
        return P @ self.r, -(P @ self.u), P @ self.d
