"""gravel.py -- the gravel bar under the logs.

Observed (braided_driftwood_bar.jpg, near and mid ground): the bar is SHINGLE -- flat plates of
grey-beige stone lying on their faces, packed and overlapping, each a light top face with a thin
dark lip on the side away from the sun (its own cast shadow on the stone below); between them
patches of smoother, darker sand/silt.

Painted (Ferrari's stone rule: light top, dark bottom row, flat ends; contrast inside the mark):

  * plates are world-space flat ellipses (so they foreshorten with the camera pitch), stacked by
    a random height; every pixel knows which plate it is on;
  * a plate's face is ONE pure step (5 lit, a few 4/6 by tilt/albedo) -- no dither on a plate;
  * the pixel(s) just past a plate's edge on the anti-sun side, not covered by a higher plate,
    are its shadow lip: step 2 (the mark's dark partner);
  * gaps and sand: step 3, a calm mid-dark field; sand patches (low-frequency) drop the plate
    density so the sand reads as a mass;
  * in a log's cast shadow everything drops by the family gap: faces 2 (3 on sun-tilted plates),
    lips 1, gaps 1.
Scale: plate size in px decides the glyph automatically (a 2-px plate is a light dash over a dark
pixel, a 6-px plate a lozenge with a lit rim).  Far away (< 1 px) it becomes a speckle of the
same three steps.
"""
import numpy as np
from paint import hash2


def _world_noise(u, v, seed):
    iu, iv = np.floor(u).astype(np.int64), np.floor(v).astype(np.int64)
    fu, fv = u - iu, v - iv
    fu = fu * fu * (3 - 2 * fu); fv = fv * fv * (3 - 2 * fv)
    a = hash2(iu, iv, seed); b = hash2(iu + 1, iv, seed)
    c = hash2(iu, iv + 1, seed); d = hash2(iu + 1, iv + 1, seed)
    return a * (1 - fu) * (1 - fv) + b * fu * (1 - fv) + c * (1 - fu) * fv + d * fu * fv


def plates(X, Y, stone_m, seed, cover=0.85, sand_scale=6.0, sand=0.35, aspect=(0.45, 0.85)):
    """per point: plate id (-1 none), its height, local (u,v) in the plate, tilt tone."""
    c = stone_m
    gx, gy = np.floor(X / c).astype(np.int64), np.floor(Y / c).astype(np.int64)
    pid = np.full(X.shape, -1, np.int64)
    ph = np.full(X.shape, -1.0)
    pu = np.zeros_like(X); pv = np.zeros_like(X)
    sandf = _world_noise(X / (c * sand_scale), Y / (c * sand_scale), seed + 77)
    for dy in (-1, 0, 1):
        for dx in (-1, 0, 1):
            cx, cy = gx + dx, gy + dy
            exists = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 11) < cover * (1 - sand * np.clip((sandf - 0.45) * 3, 0, 1))
            jx = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 1); jy = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 2)
            ra = c * (0.55 + 0.5 * hash2(cx, cy, seed + 3))
            asp = aspect[0] + (aspect[1] - aspect[0]) * hash2(cx, cy, seed + 5)
            ang = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 4) * np.pi
            px = (cx + jx) * c; py = (cy + jy) * c
            vx, vy = X - px, Y - py
            ca, sa = np.cos(ang), np.sin(ang)
            u = (vx * ca + vy * sa) / ra
            v = (-vx * sa + vy * ca) / (ra * asp)
            ins = exists & (u * u + v * v < 1.0)
            h = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 6)
            m = ins & (h > ph)
            pid = np.where(m, cx * 100003 + cy, pid)
            ph = np.where(m, h, ph)
            pu = np.where(m, u, pu); pv = np.where(m, v, pv)
    return pid, ph, pu, pv


def paint_gravel(buf, cam, light, sh, seed=0, stone_m=0.05, density=None, cover=0.85,
                 sand=0.35):
    P = buf['P']
    X, Y = P[..., 0], P[..., 1]
    pid, ph, pu, pv = plates(X, Y, stone_m, seed, cover=cover, sand=sand)
    on = pid >= 0
    # face tone: most plates at 5; tilt/albedo sends some to 4 or 6
    t = hash2(pid, 3, seed + 9)
    face = np.where(t < 0.22, 4, np.where(t > 0.88, 6, 5))
    step = np.where(on, face, 3)
    # sun-side rim: 1 px lighter where the plate edge faces the sun (only on plates >= 4 px)
    lx = light.L @ cam.r; ly = -(light.L @ cam.u)
    sdx = int(np.sign(lx)) if abs(lx) > 0.3 else 0
    sdy = int(np.sign(ly)) if abs(ly) > 0.3 else 0
    if sdy == 0 and sdx == 0:
        sdy = -1
    spx = stone_m * cam.scale
    nb_id = np.roll(np.roll(pid, -sdy, 0), -sdx, 1)
    nb_h = np.roll(np.roll(ph, -sdy, 0), -sdx, 1)
    if spx >= 4:
        rim = on & (nb_id != pid) & (nb_h < ph)
        step = np.where(rim, np.minimum(face + 1, 6), step)
    # shadow lip: the pixel on the anti-sun side of a plate edge, if the neighbour toward the sun
    # is a higher plate than this pixel's own
    up_id = np.roll(np.roll(pid, sdy, 0), sdx, 1)
    up_h = np.roll(np.roll(ph, sdy, 0), sdx, 1)
    lip = (up_id >= 0) & (up_id != pid) & (up_h > ph)
    step = np.where(lip, 2, step)
    if spx >= 7:   # thicker plates cast a 2-px lip
        up2_id = np.roll(np.roll(pid, 2 * sdy, 0), 2 * sdx, 1)
        up2_h = np.roll(np.roll(ph, 2 * sdy, 0), 2 * sdx, 1)
        lip2 = (up2_id >= 0) & (up2_id != pid) & (up2_h > ph) & ~on
        step = np.where(lip2, 2, step)
    if light.overcast:
        step = np.where(lip, 3, np.where(on, np.minimum(face, 5) - 0, 3))
        step = np.where(on & (t < 0.22), 3, step)
    # cast shadow: the family gap
    shs = np.where(on, np.where(t > 0.88, 3, 2), 1)
    shs = np.where(lip, 1, shs)
    step = np.where(sh, shs, step)
    return step.astype(int)

import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board, fit_photo
PHOTO = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg')

panels = [('photo /4', ph)]
for sc, sm in ((26, 0.05), (26, 0.08), (60, 0.05)):

    li = Light(cam, az=300, el=45)

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && cp gravel.py ../scratch/gravel_v1_glyphs.py && cat > gravel.py <<'EOF'
"""gravel.py -- the gravel bar under the logs.

Observed (braided_driftwood_bar.jpg, near and mid ground): the bar is SHINGLE -- flat plates of
grey-beige stone lying on their faces, packed and overlapping, each a light top face with a thin
dark lip on the side away from the sun (its own cast shadow on the stone below); between them
patches of smoother, darker sand/silt.

Painted (Ferrari's stone rule: light top, dark bottom row, flat ends; contrast inside the mark):

  * plates are world-space flat ellipses (so they foreshorten with the camera pitch), stacked by
    a random height; every pixel knows which plate it is on;
  * a plate's face is ONE pure step (5 lit, a few 4/6 by tilt/albedo) -- no dither on a plate;
  * the pixel(s) just past a plate's edge on the anti-sun side, not covered by a higher plate,
    are its shadow lip: step 2 (the mark's dark partner);
  * gaps and sand: step 3, a calm mid-dark field; sand patches (low-frequency) drop the plate
    density so the sand reads as a mass;
  * in a log's cast shadow everything drops by the family gap: faces 2 (3 on sun-tilted plates),
    lips 1, gaps 1.
Scale: plate size in px decides the glyph automatically (a 2-px plate is a light dash over a dark
pixel, a 6-px plate a lozenge with a lit rim).  Far away (< 1 px) it becomes a speckle of the
same three steps.
"""
import numpy as np
from paint import hash2


def _world_noise(u, v, seed):
    iu, iv = np.floor(u).astype(np.int64), np.floor(v).astype(np.int64)
    fu, fv = u - iu, v - iv
    fu = fu * fu * (3 - 2 * fu); fv = fv * fv * (3 - 2 * fv)
    a = hash2(iu, iv, seed); b = hash2(iu + 1, iv, seed)
    c = hash2(iu, iv + 1, seed); d = hash2(iu + 1, iv + 1, seed)
    return a * (1 - fu) * (1 - fv) + b * fu * (1 - fv) + c * (1 - fu) * fv + d * fu * fv


def plates(X, Y, stone_m, seed, cover=0.85, sand_scale=6.0, sand=0.35, aspect=(0.45, 0.85)):
    """per point: plate id (-1 none), its height, local (u,v) in the plate."""
    c = stone_m
    gx, gy = np.floor(X / c).astype(np.int64), np.floor(Y / c).astype(np.int64)
    pid = np.full(X.shape, -1, np.int64)
    ph = np.full(X.shape, -1.0)
    pu = np.zeros_like(X); pv = np.zeros_like(X)
    sandf = _world_noise(X / (c * sand_scale), Y / (c * sand_scale), seed + 77)
    for dy in (-1, 0, 1):
        for dx in (-1, 0, 1):
            cx, cy = gx + dx, gy + dy
            exists = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 11) < cover * (1 - sand * np.clip((sandf - 0.45) * 3, 0, 1))
            jx = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 1); jy = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 2)
            ra = c * (0.55 + 0.5 * hash2(cx, cy, seed + 3))
            asp = aspect[0] + (aspect[1] - aspect[0]) * hash2(cx, cy, seed + 5)
            ang = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 4) * np.pi
            px = (cx + jx) * c; py = (cy + jy) * c
            vx, vy = X - px, Y - py
            ca, sa = np.cos(ang), np.sin(ang)
            u = (vx * ca + vy * sa) / ra
            v = (-vx * sa + vy * ca) / (ra * asp)
            ins = exists & (u * u + v * v < 1.0)
            h = hash2(cx, cy, seed + 6)
            m = ins & (h > ph)
            pid = np.where(m, cx * 100003 + cy, pid)
            ph = np.where(m, h, ph)
            pu = np.where(m, u, pu); pv = np.where(m, v, pv)
    return pid, ph, pu, pv


def _shift(a, dy, dx, fill):
    out = np.full_like(a, fill)
    H, W = a.shape
    ys = slice(max(0, dy), H + min(0, dy)); yd = slice(max(0, -dy), H + min(0, -dy))
    xs = slice(max(0, dx), W + min(0, dx)); xd = slice(max(0, -dx), W + min(0, -dx))
    out[yd, xd] = a[ys, xs]
    return out


def paint_gravel(buf, cam, light, sh, seed=0, stone_m=0.05, density=None, cover=0.85,
                 sand=0.35):
    P = buf['P']
    X, Y = P[..., 0], P[..., 1]
    pid, ph, pu, pv = plates(X, Y, stone_m, seed, cover=cover, sand=sand)
    on = pid >= 0
    t = hash2(pid, 3, seed + 9)
    face = np.where(t < 0.22, 4, np.where(t > 0.88, 6, 5))
    step = np.where(on, face, 3)
    lx = light.L @ cam.r; ly = -(light.L @ cam.u)
    sdx = int(np.sign(lx)) if abs(lx) > 0.3 else 0
    sdy = int(np.sign(ly)) if abs(ly) > 0.3 else 0
    if sdy == 0 and sdx == 0:
        sdy = -1
    spx = stone_m * cam.scale
    # value at pixel p+ (toward the sun) / p- (away)
    tow_id = _shift(pid, sdy, sdx, -1); tow_h = _shift(ph, sdy, sdx, -1.0)
    if spx >= 4:
        away_id = _shift(pid, -sdy, -sdx, -1); away_h = _shift(ph, -sdy, -sdx, -1.0)
        rim = on & (tow_id != pid) & (tow_h < ph)
        step = np.where(rim, np.minimum(face + 1, 6), step)
    lip = (tow_id >= 0) & (tow_id != pid) & (tow_h > ph)
    step = np.where(lip, 2, step)
    if spx >= 7:
        t2_id = _shift(pid, 2 * sdy, 2 * sdx, -1); t2_h = _shift(ph, 2 * sdy, 2 * sdx, -1.0)
        lip2 = (t2_id >= 0) & (t2_id != pid) & (t2_h > ph) & ~on
        step = np.where(lip2, 2, step)
    if light.overcast:
        step = np.where(on, np.where(t < 0.22, 3, np.where(t > 0.88, 5, 4)), 3)
        step = np.where(lip, 2, step)
    shs = np.where(on, np.where(t > 0.88, 3, 2), 1)
    shs = np.where(lip, 1, shs)
    step = np.where(sh, shs, step)
    return step.astype(int)

Bash: Render end-type study
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && cat > s1_ends.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 1b: the ends -- sawn, snapped, splintered -- at two sizes."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')


def end_cell(kind, scale=60.0, W=64, H=48, az=300, el=40, seed=4, yaw_log=200, r=0.22, pitch=25):
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, look=(0.25, 0, 0.1))
    a = np.radians(yaw_log)
    ax = np.array([np.cos(a), np.sin(a), 0.0])
    # the END is at the image centre, the log recedes behind it
    p1 = np.array([0.0, 0.0, r - 0.02]); p0 = p1 - ax * 2.5
    lg = geo.Log([p0 + [0, 0, 0.0], p1], [r * 1.05, r], ends=('sawn', kind), seed=seed)
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el)
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, li, seed=seed)
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps()), cv, buf


if __name__ == '__main__':
    P = []
    for k in ('sawn', 'snapped', 'splinter'):
        for sc in (60, 30):
            P.append((f'{k} {sc}px/m', end_cell(k, scale=sc)[0]))
    board(P, scale=6, cols=2, title='s1 ends').save(f'{ROOT}/scratch/s1_ends.png')
EOF
python3 s1_ends.py

Bash: Rebuild jagged ends as teeth primitives
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('geo.py').read()
a=s.index("        # jagged-end profiles"); b=s.index("        # stub primitives")
s=s[:a]+'''        # jagged ends: an oblique base cut plus teeth (tapered round cones) standing out of the
        # rim along the axis.  snapped = few broad short teeth, a tilted break;
        # splinter = many long thin spikes.  Teeth thinner than ~1 px are drawn as strokes later.
        self.jag = []           # per end: (tilt normal offset vector, base setback)
        self.teeth = []         # (a, b, r1, r2, end)
        for e in (0, 1):
            kind_e = ends[e]
            c, out = self.ends_frames()[e]
            r_end = self.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1]
            up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
            e1 = norm(np.cross(up, out)); e2 = np.cross(out, e1)
            if kind_e == 'sawn':
                self.jag.append((out, 0.0))
                continue
            tilt = rng.uniform(0.15, 0.5) * (1 if kind_e == 'snapped' else 0.6)
            ta = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
            nrm = norm(out + tilt * (np.cos(ta) * e1 + np.sin(ta) * e2))
            setback = r_end * (0.25 if kind_e == 'snapped' else 0.5)
            self.jag.append((nrm, setback))
            nt = rng.integers(3, 6) if kind_e == 'snapped' else rng.integers(7, 13)
            for _ in range(nt):
                ang = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
                rr = r_end * rng.uniform(0.35, 0.85)
                base = c - out * setback * 1.8 + rr * (np.cos(ang) * e1 + np.sin(ang) * e2)
                if kind_e == 'snapped':
                    ln = r_end * rng.uniform(0.4, 1.0); r1 = r_end * rng.uniform(0.22, 0.38)
                else:
                    ln = r_end * rng.uniform(0.8, 2.6); r1 = r_end * rng.uniform(0.07, 0.2)
                inward = -(np.cos(ang) * e1 + np.sin(ang) * e2) * rng.uniform(0.0, 0.25)
                tip = base + norm(out + inward) * (ln + setback * 1.8)
                self.teeth.append((base, tip, r1, r1 * 0.15, e))
'''+s[b:]
# cut(): use plane with setback
a=s.index("    def cut(self, p, d):"); b=s.index("    def sdf(self, p, with_parts=False):")
s=s[:a]+'''    def cut(self, p, d):
        """intersect with the end half-spaces (oblique and set back for broken ends)."""
        parts = np.zeros(p.shape[:-1], int)
        for e, ((c, out), (nrm, setback)) in enumerate(zip(self.ends_frames(), self.jag)):
            dc = (p - (c - out * setback)) @ nrm
            parts = np.where(dc > d, e + 1, parts)
            d = np.maximum(d, dc)
        return d, parts

'''+s[b:]
s=s.replace("""        d, parts = self.cut(p, d)
        for (a, b, r1, r2) in self.stub_prims:""","""        d, parts = self.cut(p, d)
        for (a, b, r1, r2, e) in self.teeth:
            dt = sd_round_cone(p, a, b, r1, max(r2, 1e-4))
            # a tooth is only the part of the cone inside the log's cylinder envelope
            parts = np.where(dt < d, 5, parts)
            d = np.minimum(d, dt)
        for (a, b, r1, r2) in self.stub_prims:""")
s=s.replace("""        for (a, b, r1, r2) in self.stub_prims:
            lo = np.minimum""","""        for (a, b, r1, r2, e) in self.teeth:
            lo = np.minimum(lo, np.minimum(a, b) - r1); hi = np.maximum(hi, np.maximum(a, b) + r1)
        for (a, b, r1, r2) in self.stub_prims:
            lo = np.minimum""")
open('geo.py','w').write(s)

39. elements/driftwood/scratch/s1_ends.png

Bash: Jagged ends via Lipschitz-bounded angular profile
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('geo.py').read()
a=s.index("        # jagged ends: an oblique base cut"); b=s.index("        # stub primitives")
s=s[:a]+'''        # jagged ends: the shell of the trunk stands out further in some angular sectors (the
        # teeth of the break), and the break surface between them falls toward the axis.
        # profile(theta) = tooth height; scaled by radial fraction so the break face is a rough
        # cone.  snapped = 3-5 broad teeth; splinter = 6-10 narrower, longer teeth plus stroke
        # spikes (drawn in screen space later, since they are < 1 px wide).
        self.jag = []
        self.spikes = []        # (base point, direction, length, end) for stroke splinters
        ang = np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, 256, endpoint=False)
        for e in (0, 1):
            kind_e = ends[e]
            prof = np.zeros(256)
            r_end = self.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1]
            if kind_e in ('snapped', 'splinter'):
                nt = rng.integers(3, 6) if kind_e == 'snapped' else rng.integers(6, 10)
                for _ in range(nt):
                    cang = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
                    w = rng.uniform(0.5, 1.1) if kind_e == 'snapped' else rng.uniform(0.35, 0.7)
                    h = r_end * (rng.uniform(0.35, 1.0) if kind_e == 'snapped' else rng.uniform(0.6, 2.0))
                    dd = np.abs(np.angle(np.exp(1j * (ang - cang))))
                    prof = np.maximum(prof, h * np.clip(1 - dd / w, 0, 1))
                prof += r_end * 0.08 * rng.random(256)
                c, out = self.ends_frames()[e]
                up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
                e1 = norm(np.cross(up, out)); e2 = np.cross(out, e1)
                if kind_e == 'splinter':
                    for _ in range(rng.integers(4, 9)):
                        th = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi); rr = r_end * rng.uniform(0.3, 0.95)
                        base = c + rr * (np.cos(th) * e1 + np.sin(th) * e2)
                        k = int(th / (2 * np.pi) * 256) % 256
                        ln = prof[k] * rr / r_end + r_end * rng.uniform(0.5, 1.6)
                        dirv = norm(out + rng.normal(0, 0.12, 3))
                        self.spikes.append((base, dirv, ln, e))
            lip = 1.0 + np.abs(np.diff(np.concatenate([prof, prof[:1]]))).max() / (2 * np.pi / 256) / max(r_end, 1e-6)
            self.jag.append((prof, lip))
'''+s[b:]
a=s.index("    def cut(self, p, d):"); b=s.index("    def sdf(self, p, with_parts=False):")
s=s[:a]+'''    def cut(self, p, d):
        """intersect with the end half-spaces; broken ends raise the cut by the tooth profile,
        scaled by radial fraction.  The cut distance is divided by its Lipschitz bound so sphere
        tracing stays conservative."""
        parts = np.zeros(p.shape[:-1], int)
        for e, ((c, out), (prof, lip)) in enumerate(zip(self.ends_frames(), self.jag)):
            r_end = self.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1]
            up = np.array([0, 0, 1.0])
            e1 = norm(np.cross(up, out)); e2 = np.cross(out, e1)
            v = p - c
            th = np.arctan2(v @ e1, v @ e2)
            rho = np.sqrt((v @ e1) ** 2 + (v @ e2) ** 2) / r_end
            idx = ((th % (2 * np.pi)) / (2 * np.pi) * len(prof)).astype(int) % len(prof)
            lim = prof[idx] * np.clip(rho, 0, 1) ** 1.5
            dc = (v @ out - lim) / (lip * 1.6 if prof.max() > 0 else 1.0)
            parts = np.where(dc > d, e + 1, parts)
            d = np.maximum(d, dc)
        return d, parts

'''+s[b:]
# remove teeth loops
s=s.replace("""        for (a, b, r1, r2, e) in self.teeth:
            dt = sd_round_cone(p, a, b, r1, max(r2, 1e-4))
            # a tooth is only the part of the cone inside the log's cylinder envelope
            parts = np.where(dt < d, 5, parts)
            d = np.minimum(d, dt)
""","")
s=s.replace("""        for (a, b, r1, r2, e) in self.teeth:
            lo = np.minimum(lo, np.minimum(a, b) - r1); hi = np.maximum(hi, np.maximum(a, b) + r1)
""","")
s=s.replace("b = b + ax * (3.5 * self.rad[-1])","b = b + ax * (4.0 * self.rad[-1])")
s=s.replace("a = a - ax * (3.5 * self.rad[0])","a = a - ax * (4.0 * self.rad[0])")
s=s.replace("    def trace(cam, logs, steps=96):","    def trace(cam, logs, steps=160):")
s=s.replace("def trace(cam, logs, steps=96):","def trace(cam, logs, steps=200):")
open('geo.py','w').write(s)

Bash: Paint sawn and broken end faces
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
r=open('render.py').read()
a=r.index("    # end faces (sawn): a flat plane"); b=r.index("    cv.mat[wood] = WOOD")
r=r[:a]+'''    # ---------- end faces
    st = end_faces(st, buf, logs, light, sh, cam)
'''+r[b:]
r=r.replace("def gravel_steps(", '''def end_faces(st, buf, logs, light, sh, cam):
    """sawn: a flat face -- ONE lit step (6) for the plane, a 1-px darker ring at the rounded
    arris (4), growth rings as 1-px runs one step down (5) when the face is >= 16 px, a radial
    check (3) from the pith when >= 12 px.  In shadow the same marks one family down.
    broken: raw wood by its facet normal -- lit facets 6/7, turned facets 4, shadowed 3 --
    the palest thing on the log (raw wood has not silvered)."""
    obj = buf['obj']; part = buf['part']
    ndl = (buf['N'] * light.L).sum(-1)
    out = st.copy()
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        for e in (0, 1):
            m = (obj == k) & (part == e + 1)
            if not m.any():
                continue
            r_px = lg.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1] * cam.scale
            lit = (ndl > 0.2) & ~sh & ~light.overcast
            if lg.ends[e] == 'sawn':
                base = np.where(lit, 6, 3)
                if light.overcast:
                    base = np.full(base.shape, 5)
                rho = buf['rho']
                v = base.copy()
                if r_px >= 3:
                    arris = rho > 1 - 1.0 / max(r_px, 1)
                    v = np.where(arris, base - 2, v)
                if r_px >= 8:
                    ring = (np.abs(((rho * r_px / 3.2) % 1.0) - 0.5) < 0.16) & (rho < 0.85)
                    v = np.where(ring, base - 1, v)
                if r_px >= 6:
                    th = buf['th']
                    ca = hash_angle(lg.seed + e)
                    chk = (np.abs(np.angle(np.exp(1j * (th - ca)))) * rho * r_px < 0.6) & (rho > 0.12)
                    v = np.where(chk, base - 3, v)
                out = np.where(m, v, out)
            else:
                v = np.where(lit, np.where(ndl > 0.7, 7, 6), np.where(ndl > 0.0, 4, 3))
                if light.overcast:
                    v = np.where(buf['N'][..., 2] > 0.3, 6, 4)
                out = np.where(m, v, out)
    return out


def hash_angle(s):
    return (np.sin(s * 12.9898) * 43758.5453) % 1.0 * 2 * np.pi

"""Stage 1c: the same log from 120 px long down to a few pixels."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')


def size_cell(length_px, W=None, H=None, az=300, el=40, pitch=30, seed=5, yaw_log=12, L=3.0, r=0.16,
              ends=('sawn', 'snapped')):
    scale = length_px / L
    W = W or int(length_px * 1.15) + 8; H = H or max(12, int(length_px * 0.45) + 6)
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, look=(0, 0, 0.1))
    a = np.radians(yaw_log); ax = np.array([np.cos(a), np.sin(a), 0.0])
    lg = geo.Log([-ax * L / 2 + [0, 0, r - 0.02], ax * L / 2 + [0, 0, r * 0.8 - 0.02]], [r, r * 0.8],
                 ends=ends, seed=seed)
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el)
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, li, seed=seed, stone_m=0.06)
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())


if __name__ == '__main__':
    P = [(f'{n} px long', size_cell(n)) for n in (120, 60, 32, 16, 8, 4)]
    board(P, scale=4, cols=2, title='s1 sizes (native x4)').save(f'{ROOT}/scratch/s1_sizes.png')
EOF

Bash: Add thin-log stroke glyphs
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
r=open('render.py').read()
r=r.replace("""    # ---------- drawing: contact line and the log's bottom row
    if o['contact']:
        contact(cv, buf, logs, cam)
    return cv, buf, sh""","""    # ---------- drawing: contact line and the log's bottom row
    if o['contact']:
        contact(cv, buf, logs, cam)
    # ---------- thin logs: explicit stroke glyphs (the classes collapse below ~4.5 px)
    thin_logs(cv, buf, logs, cam, light, sh)
    return cv, buf, sh


THIN_ROWS = {1: [6], 2: [6, 2], 3: [6, 4, 2], 4: [5, 6, 4, 2]}
THIN_ROWS_SH = {1: [3], 2: [3, 1], 3: [3, 2, 1], 4: [3, 3, 2, 1]}


def thin_logs(cv, buf, logs, cam, light, sh):
    \"\"\"trigger: a log whose projected diameter is < 4.5 px.  recipe (Ferrari's narrow limbs:
    the classes shrink to one pixel each, then drop from the shadow side): the axis is drawn as a
    clean-run line, t = round(diameter) pixels thick; rows from the lit side:
        1: light          2: light, core          3: light, half, core
        4: edge, light, half, core
    and a darkest contact pixel under each column (the smallest light-top / dark-bottom pair).
    A lit sawn end adds one end-face pixel (7).\"\"\"
    H, W = cv.step.shape
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        dmax = 2 * lg.rad.max() * cam.scale
        if dmax >= 4.5:
            continue
        # clear what the tracer drew (it aliases at this size)
        m = buf['obj'] == k
        cv.mat[m] = GRAVEL
        sx, sy = cam.project(lg.pts)
        for i in range(len(lg.pts) - 1):
            x0, y0, x1, y1 = sx[i], sy[i], sx[i + 1], sy[i + 1]
            d0 = 2 * lg.rad[i] * cam.scale; d1 = 2 * lg.rad[i + 1] * cam.scale
            # top of the silhouette: axis raised by half the projected thickness
            n = int(max(abs(x1 - x0), abs(y1 - y0))) + 1
            for j in range(n):
                f = j / max(n - 1, 1)
                x = x0 + (x1 - x0) * f; y = y0 + (y1 - y0) * f
                dd = d0 + (d1 - d0) * f
                t = int(np.clip(round(dd * 0.9), 1, 4))
                xi = int(np.floor(x)); yt = int(np.floor(y - dd * 0.5))
                if not (0 <= xi < W):
                    continue
                shd = 0 <= yt < H and sh[yt, xi]
                rows = (THIN_ROWS_SH if (shd or light.overcast) else THIN_ROWS)[t]
                for rr, v in enumerate(rows):
                    yy = yt + rr
                    if 0 <= yy < H:
                        cv.mat[yy, xi] = WOOD; cv.step[yy, xi] = v
                yy = yt + len(rows)
                if 0 <= yy < H:
                    cv.mat[yy, xi] = GRAVEL; cv.step[yy, xi] = 0
        # sawn end facing the sun: one bright pixel
        for e in (0, 1):
            if lg.ends[e] == 'sawn':
                c, out = lg.ends_frames()[e]
                if out @ light.L > 0.3 and not light.overcast:
                    ex, ey = cam.project(c)
                    xi, yi = int(ex[0]), int(ey[0] - lg.rad[0] * cam.scale * 0.5)
                    if 0 <= xi < W and 0 <= yi < H:
                        cv.mat[yi, xi] = WOOD; cv.step[yi, xi] = 7""")
open('render.py','w').write(r)

# Critique (newest first)

## Round 1 — 2026-09-25 21:22 — stage 0 `stages/s0a_pale_trunk_8x.png`, `s0a2_pipeline_copy_8x.png`; stage 1 `stages/s1_lights_4x.png`
**Verdict: stage 0 REVISE (the test was too easy to count); stage 1 REVISE.** Note that you moved on to stage 1 before you had a verdict on stage 0. (My read; your call.)

### Stage 0 (V09 pale trunk)
What's working: inside the trunk, the ramp and ordered-dither bands match his almost class for class. s0a2, which gets there from a lit 3-D cylinder rather than a fit, is the more valuable result, because it will rotate to lie down.

Why it doesn't count yet:

- **The silhouette and the background are his.** Both scripts take his left and right silhouette columns and paste your interior into his frame. So the only thing tested was the cylinder ramp, and a smooth cylinder is the easiest passage in V09.
- **The one place you had to draw, you missed.** At the top of `s0a_pale_trunk_8x.png` (right panel, top ~15 rows), his limb leaves the trunk as a separate 2–3 px branch going up-left, with a lit upper edge and sky showing in the crotch. Your fill turns it into a fat blunt blob fused to the trunk.
- **Wrong passage for driftwood.** Driftwood is silvered wood with dark longitudinal checks, split ends, knots, and broken stubs. The smooth trunk teaches none of that. Copy a passage that has checks and an end: V09's big foreground trunks (bark with vertical dark strokes), the mossy fallen log at the bottom right of V09, or the fallen trunks in V02. Draw the silhouette yourself.

### Stage 1 (one log, six lights)
Viewing crop: `_critic/r1_drift_s1_left_8x.png` (left end of "left 40", 8×).

- **It reads as a steel pipe, not a log.** It's a perfectly straight cylinder of constant diameter with a flat square cap for an end. In your refs (`openverse_Driftwood_on_Gravel_bar_*`, `openverse_Driftwood_log_on_sand_in_wind_*`) every log tapers, bends slightly, and has a broken or rounded end, stubs, and dark checks running along the grain. Even in greyscale stage 1, the *form* has to be a log: taper, a slight sag, and an end with a readable cross-section (a lighter end-grain face with a dark rim).
- **The body is a single 50% checker band from end to end** between a 1 px lit rim and a dark bottom row. At 8× it's three stripes. There's no core shadow on the turn and no reflected light under it, and the checker density never changes along the log.
- **Contact.** The log floats. A log on gravel needs a 1 px darkest contact line and a pocket of black under the belly where it lifts off the ground. The cast shadow in "left 40" and "right 40" is a smudge of a few pixels.
- **The ground is TV static.** White-noise grey fights the log at every scale and is the loudest thing in the sheet. Use a flat field or a calm, plain gravel texture for form studies, so the only thing speaking is the log.
- The lighting variants do their job (front-lit reads flat, back-lit goes dark with the

Write: elements/driftwood/src/s0b_fallen.py
"""Stage 0B: master copy of Ferrari's fallen dead tree (Living Worlds V02, dusk, native crop
x 250..420, y 358..412).  The silhouette is MY sketch (polyline + widths read off his image as a
copyist would, then drawn by my own rasteriser), not his mask.

Procedure found:

  * contre-jour: the whole body is ONE near-black class (69).  No form inside the mass.
  * the only light is a rim (71, dull maroon) on the silhouette facing the glow (up-right):
      - on the trunk's top contour: a run of rim pixels ALONG the contour, 1 px thick
        (2 px where the contour is near-horizontal: the rim sits on top of the run);
      - on each twig (2 px black) the rim is the single right-hand pixel: '##r'.
  * twigs are 2-px strokes stepping in clean runs (1:1, 1:2, 2:1), forking in V/Y shapes, with
    a 1-px lighter-than-sky gap nowhere -- they are pure silhouette.
  * the trunk contour is a sequence of runs that shorten smoothly round the curve (14,10,6,5,4,
    4,4,3,3,3,2,2 ...), never jittering.
"""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from s0a_pale_trunk import load, ROOT
from util import board

X0, Y0, X1, Y1 = 250, 358, 420, 412
BODY, RIM = 69, 71

# copyist's sketch (native coords): trunk centreline with widths; twigs as polylines (2 px)
TRUNK = [(258, 391, 11), (266, 399, 15), (290, 394, 14), (315, 389, 10), (338, 386, 6),
         (352, 386, 4), (372, 390, 4), (395, 398, 4), (420, 409, 4)]
TWIGS = [
    [(262, 394), (257, 386), (251, 382)],
    [(270, 390), (270, 380), (266, 375), (263, 371)],
    [(276, 388), (282, 380), (289, 377)],
    [(290, 390), (291, 380), (295, 372), (293, 366)],
    [(300, 386), (305, 378), (307, 370), (312, 362)],
    [(310, 384), (317, 376), (318, 366)],
    [(318, 386), (325, 378), (333, 373), (340, 372)],
    [(330, 384), (336, 376), (336, 367), (340, 362)],
    [(345, 384), (349, 376), (356, 368), (362, 367)],
    [(355, 385), (362, 377), (370, 373)],
    [(360, 387), (360, 377), (363, 366), (370, 364)],
]


def line_px(p, q):
    """clean-run line between two points: a DDA on the major axis, rounded minor axis."""
    (x0, y0), (x1, y1) = p, q
    n = int(max(abs(x1 - x0), abs(y1 - y0))) + 1
    t = np.linspace(0, 1, n)
    return np.round(x0 + (x1 - x0) * t).astype(int), np.round(y0 + (y1 - y0) * t).astype(int)


def draw(H, W, snap=True):
    M = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    # trunk: for each column, a vertical span centred on the interpolated centreline
    xs = np.array([p[0] for p in TRUNK]); ys = np.array([p[1] for p in TRUNK])
    ws = np.array([p[2] for p in TRUNK])
    for x in range(int(xs[0]), int(xs[-1]) + 1):
        cy = np.interp(x, xs, ys); w = np.interp(x, xs, ws)
        top = int(round(cy - w / 2)); bot = int(round(cy + w / 2)) - 1
        for y in range(top, bot + 1):
            if 0 <= y - Y0 < H and 0 <= x - X0 < W:
                M[y - Y0, x - X0] = True
    # twigs: 2-px strokes (the stroke and its right neighbour)
    for tw in TWIGS:
        for a, b in zip(tw[:-1], tw[1:]):
            lx, ly = line_px(a, b)
            for x, y in zip(lx, ly):
                for dx in (0, 1):
                    if 0 <= y - Y0 < H and 0 <= x + dx - X0 < W:
                        M[y - Y0, x + dx - X0] = True
    return M


def rim(M):
    """rim = pixels of the mass exposed to the right, or exposed upward where the contour faces
    up-right (the pixel up-left is also mass or the one up-right is empty)."""
    P = np.pad(M, 1)
    right_open = M & ~P[1:-1, 2:]
    up_open = M & ~P[:-2, 1:-1]
    upright_open = M & ~P[:-2, 2:]
    R = right_open | (up_open & upright_open & P[1:-1, :-2])
    return R


def main():
    d = load('V02'); idx = d['idx']; pal = d['pal']
    ref = idx[Y0:Y1, X0:X1]
    H, W = ref.shape
    M = draw(H, W)
    R = rim(M)
    mine = ref.copy()
    refmass = np.isin(ref, [69, 70, 223, 71, 72, 73])
    mine[refmass] = ref[~refmass][0] if (~refmass).any() else ref[0, 0]
    # repaint the background under his mass with the nearest non-mass pixel above (so only my
    # silhouette is shown)
    for x in range(W):
        last = None
        for y in range(H):
            if refmass[y, x]:
                if last is not None:
                    mine[y, x] = last
            else:
                last = ref[y, x]
    mine[M] = BODY
    mine[R] = RIM
    A = pal[ref]; B = pal[mine]
    iou = (M & refmass).sum() / (M | refmass).sum()
    rimref = np.isin(ref, [71, 72, 73])
    print(f'silhouette IoU {iou:.2f}; rim pixels ref {rimref.sum()} mine {R.sum()}; '
          f'rim on rim {(R & rimref).sum()}')
    board([('Ferrari V02 (native 170x54)', A), ('my copy: sketch + body/rim rules', B)], scale=6, cols=1,
          title='s0b fallen dead tree, contre-jour').save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s0b_fallen_6x.png')


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

Bash: Derive coarse sketch from skeleton, redraw by rules
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('s0b_fallen.py').read()
s=s.replace("X0, Y0, X1, Y1 = 250, 358, 420, 412","X0, Y0, X1, Y1 = 250, 358, 362, 412")
a=s.index("# copyist's sketch"); b=s.index("def line_px")
s=s[:a]+'''

def sketch_from(ref):
    """the copyist's sketch, made the way a copyist makes one: the big shape reduced to a few
    vertices.  Trunk: centre and width sampled every 8 columns of the thick mass.  Twigs: the
    skeleton of the thin mass, traced into paths and simplified (Douglas-Peucker, 1.2 px), so
    only the gesture survives; the strokes themselves are redrawn by my rules."""
    from scipy import ndimage as ndi
    from skimage.morphology import skeletonize
    from skimage.measure import approximate_polygon
    mass = np.isin(ref, [69, 70, 223, 71, 72, 73])
    thick = ndi.binary_opening(mass, np.ones((5, 5)))
    trunk = []
    for x in range(0, ref.shape[1], 8):
        ys = np.nonzero(thick[:, x])[0]
        if len(ys) >= 3:
            trunk.append((x + X0, Y0 + 0.5 * (ys.min() + ys.max() + 1), ys.max() - ys.min() + 1))
    thin = mass & ~ndi.binary_dilation(thick, iterations=1)
    sk = skeletonize(thin)
    lab, n = ndi.label(sk, np.ones((3, 3)))
    twigs = []
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(lab == i)
        if len(ys) < 4:
            continue
        # order the pixels by walking from an endpoint (greedy nearest neighbour)
        pts = list(zip(xs, ys)); path = [pts.pop(int(np.argmax(ys)))]
        while pts:
            d = [abs(p[0] - path[-1][0]) + abs(p[1] - path[-1][1]) for p in pts]
            j = int(np.argmin(d))
            if d[j] > 3:
                break
            path.append(pts.pop(j))
        poly = approximate_polygon(np.array(path, float), tolerance=1.2)
        twigs.append([(p[0] + X0, p[1] + Y0) for p in poly])
    return trunk, twigs


TRUNK, TWIGS = None, None


'''+s[b:]
s=s.replace("""    ref = idx[Y0:Y1, X0:X1]
    H, W = ref.shape
    M = draw(H, W)""","""    ref = idx[Y0:Y1, X0:X1]
    H, W = ref.shape
    global TRUNK, TWIGS
    TRUNK, TWIGS = sketch_from(ref)
    print('sketch:', len(TRUNK), 'trunk samples,', len(TWIGS), 'twigs,', sum(len(t) for t in TWIGS), 'vertices')
    M = draw(H, W)""")
open('s0b_fallen.py','w').write(s)

s=open('geo.py').read()
# worn ends: no cut

            r_end = self.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1]""","""        for e, ((c, out), (prof, lip)) in enumerate(zip(self.ends_frames(), self.jag)):
            if self.ends[e] == 'worn':
                continue
            r_end = self.rad[0 if e == 0 else -1]""")

                a = a - ax * (4.0 * self.rad[0])
            if i == n - 2:
                b = b + ax * (4.0 * self.rad[-1])""","""            if i == 0 and self.ends[0] != 'worn':
                a = a - ax * (4.0 * self.rad[0])
            if i == n - 2 and self.ends[1] != 'worn':
                b = b + ax * (4.0 * self.rad[-1])""")
s+='''

def make_log(L=3.0, r=0.16, yaw=0.0, centre=(0, 0), taper=0.75, bend=0.06, sag=0.03, lumps=0.08,
             nseg=7, ends=('sawn', 'snapped'), seed=0, sink=0.015, stubs=(), rootwad=None,
             kind='drift', wet=0.0, lift=None):
    """a driftwood log lying on the ground: a gently bent, tapering, lumpy axis.
    bend: lateral bow (fraction of L); sag: vertical bow; lumps: radius noise (fraction).
    The log is lowered until its lowest point touches the ground (minus sink), so a bowed log
    rests on two contacts and lifts off between them -- the belly pocket."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t = np.linspace(-0.5, 0.5, nseg)
    ph = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
    lat = bend * L * (np.sin(np.pi * (t + 0.5)) * rng.choice([-1, 1]) + 0.3 * np.sin(2 * np.pi * t + ph))
    vert = sag * L * np.sin(np.pi * (t + 0.5)) * rng.choice([-1, 1])
    rad = r * (1 + (taper - 1) * (t + 0.5)) * (1 + lumps * (rng.random(nseg) - 0.5) * 2)
    a = np.radians(yaw)
    ax = np.array([np.cos(a), np.sin(a), 0.0]); side = np.array([-np.sin(a), np.cos(a), 0.0])
    pts = np.array([centre[0], centre[1], 0.0]) + np.outer(t * L, ax) + np.outer(lat, side)
    pts[:, 2] = rad + vert
    if lift is not None:
        pts[:, 2] += np.interp(t, [-0.5, 0.5], lift)
    pts[:, 2] -= (pts[:, 2] - rad).min() + sink
    return Log(pts, rad, ends=ends, stubs=stubs, rootwad=rootwad, seed=seed, kind=kind, wet=wet)


def belly(buf, logs, cam):
    """ground pixels that lie UNDER a log (a vertical ray from the ground hits it): the pocket
    where the log lifts off the gravel."""
    P = buf['P']; g = buf['obj'] == -1
    out = np.zeros(g.shape, bool)
    if not logs:
        return out
    p = P[g]
    hit = np.zeros(len(p), bool)
    hmax = max(2 * lg.rad.max() for lg in logs)
    for h in np.linspace(0.01, hmax, 10):
        q = p + np.array([0, 0, h])
        for lg in logs:
            hit |= lg.sdf(q) < 0
    out[g] = hit
    return out
'''
open('geo.py','w').write(s)

r=open('render.py').read()

    pocket = geo.belly(buf, logs, cam)
    cv.step[pocket & (cv.mat == GRAVEL)] = np.minimum(cv.step[pocket & (cv.mat == GRAVEL)], 0)
    return _contact_line(cv, buf, logs, cam)


def _contact_line(cv, buf, logs, cam):""")

"""Stage 1 hero: one log in greyscale; photo | pixels | Ferrari at 8x, plus the light sweep."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board, fit_photo
from s0a_pale_trunk import load

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')
PHOTO = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg')


def hero(az=300, el=40, W=150, H=70, scale=40, pitch=30, seed=3, ends=('sawn', 'snapped'), yaw=10,
         L=3.2, r=0.17, flat=False, **kw):
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch)
    lg = geo.make_log(L=L, r=r, yaw=yaw, seed=seed, ends=ends, **kw)
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, Light(cam, az=az, el=el), seed=2, stone_m=0.06,
                                opts=dict(gravel=not flat))
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())


if __name__ == '__main__':
    a = hero()
    board([('left-front 40, 8x crop', a[15:60, 0:75]), ('right half', a[15:60, 75:150])], scale=8, cols=1).save(f'{ROOT}/scratch/s1_hero8.png')

Bash: Limit belly pocket to small clearance
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('geo.py').read()
a=s.index("def belly(buf, logs, cam):")
s=s[:a]+'''def belly(buf, logs, cam, max_gap_px=2.5):
    """ground pixels UNDER a log with a small clearance (a vertical ray from the ground meets
    the log within max_gap_px): the dark pocket where a log lifts off the gravel.  Larger gaps
    are simply ground in the log's shadow."""
    P = buf['P']; g = buf['obj'] == -1
    out = np.zeros(g.shape, bool)
    if not logs:
        return out
    p = P[g]
    hit = np.zeros(len(p), bool)
    hmax = max_gap_px / cam.scale
    for h in np.linspace(0.2, 1.0, 5) * hmax:
        q = p + np.array([0, 0, h])
        for lg in logs:
            hit |= lg.sdf(q) < 0
    out[g] = hit
    return out
'''
open('geo.py','w').write(s)
EOF

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && cat > s1_log.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 1: one log in greyscale -- lights, ends, sizes.  Writes stages/s1_*.png"""
import sys, os, time
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')


def cell(az, el, flat=False, W=150, H=70, scale=40, pitch=30, seed=3, ends=('sawn', 'snapped'), yaw=10,
         L=3.2, r=0.17, overcast=False, look=(0, 0, 0), **kw):
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, look=look)
    lg = geo.make_log(L=L, r=r, yaw=yaw, seed=seed, ends=ends, **kw)
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el, overcast=overcast)
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, li, seed=2, stone_m=0.06, opts=dict(gravel=not flat))
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())


LIGHTS = [('left-front 40', 300, 40, False), ('right-front 40', 60, 40, False),
          ('back-left 30', 220, 30, False), ('back-right 25', 150, 25, False),
          ('high 70', 330, 70, False), ('overcast', 0, 80, True)]

if __name__ == '__main__':
    t = time.time()
    for flat in (True, False):
        P = [(n, cell(a, e, flat=flat, overcast=o)) for n, a, e, o in LIGHTS]
        tag = 'flat' if flat else 'gravel'
        board(P, scale=4, cols=2, title=f's1 one log, six lights ({tag} ground), 30 deg, 40 px/m, native x4'
              ).save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s1_lights_{tag}_4x.png')
    # ends: each end at the centre, two sizes
    P = []
    for k in ('sawn', 'worn', 'snapped', 'splinter'):
        for sc in (60, 30):
            P.append((f'{k} end, {sc} px/m', cell(300, 40, flat=True, W=70, H=50, scale=sc, yaw=195, L=2.4,
                                                   r=0.2, ends=('sawn', k), look=(-1.1 if sc == 60 else -0.9, -0.3, 0), seed=5)))
    board(P, scale=5, cols=2, title='s1 ends (left-front sun), native x5').save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s1_ends_5x.png')
    # sizes
    P = []
    for n in (120, 60, 32, 16, 8, 4):
        sc = n / 3.2
        P.append((f'{n} px long', cell(300, 40, flat=False, W=int(n * 1.2) + 10, H=max(14, int(n * 0.45) + 8),
                                       scale=sc)))
    board(P, scale=4, cols=3, title='s1 sizes, native x4').save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s1_sizes_4x.png')
    print('done', time.time() - t)
EOF

Bash: Despeckle break faces, smooth profile
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('geo.py').read()
s=s.replace("                prof += r_end * 0.08 * rng.random(256)\n","")

r=open('render.py').read()
r=r.replace('''def hash_angle(s):''','''def despeckle(st, mask, passes=2):
    """no orphan pixels inside a region: a pixel unlike all four neighbours takes the most common
    neighbour value (only within mask)."""
    st = st.copy()
    H, W = st.shape
    for _ in range(passes):
        nb = np.stack([np.roll(st, 1, 0), np.roll(st, -1, 0), np.roll(st, 1, 1), np.roll(st, -1, 1)])
        orphan = mask & np.all(nb != st[None], axis=0)
        if not orphan.any():
            break
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(orphan)
        for y, x in zip(ys, xs):
            vals = nb[:, y, x]
            u, c = np.unique(vals, return_counts=True)
            st[y, x] = u[np.argmax(c)]
    return st


def hash_angle(s):''')
r=r.replace("""                out = np.where(m, v, out)
    return out""","""                out = np.where(m, v, out)
                out = despeckle(out, m)
    return out""")
open('render.py','w').write(r)
EOF

# output:
## Round 2 — 2026-09-25 21:34 — stage 0 `stages/s0b_fallen_6x.png` (V02 fallen tree); stage 1 `stages/s1_compare_8x.png`
**Stage 0: REVISE. Stage 1: REVISE.** Good choice of passage for s0b: a gnarled dead tree drawn by hand is the right test. (My read; your call.)

### s0b fallen tree
What's working: the value plan (a near-black trunk against the violet field) and the placement of the main uprights on the right are close.
What gives it away at 6×:

1. **The branches are straight sticks of even thickness.** His branches kink every few pixels, thicken at the joints, fork into little knobbed crooks, and run 2–3 px thick near the trunk, tapering to 1 px. Yours are mostly 1–2 px straight runs. The left cluster (his x≈40–130: three crooked uprights with a crossing limb) is nearly gone in yours, leaving faint disconnected ticks.
2. **The branches are disconnected.** The branch starting around x≈200 in your copy floats free of the trunk; in his every branch is attached. A floating stick is the fastest tell.
3. **The trunk's upper edge is a ruled staircase** at the left end (your x≈40–220, y≈570–640). It steps down in equal runs, where his top edge is a smooth convex arc with a thin maroon rim light running along it.
4. **The rim light.** His whole silhouette carries a 1 px dark-maroon rim where the backlight wraps it: branches, trunk top, everything. Yours has it in patches.

### Stage 1 (`s1_compare_8x.png`)
Better: there's a taper now and a contact line. But:

- **It reads as a crayon or a stick of chalk.** The end is a flat, pure-white ellipse, brighter than anything else in the frame, on a straight tapered cylinder. In your photo (left panel) the log bends, thickens toward a broken, rounded end, and the end face has about the body's value with dark checks and a darker rim, not a white disc.
- **The lit top edge is a sawtooth** of alternating light pixels climbing the diagonal, on a regular period. That's the fastest mechanical tell in the image. On a diagonal a lit edge is a clean run (2:1 or 3:1 steps) of one tone.
- **The ground is still TV static,** and it's still the loudest thing in the frame. Use a plain field.
- No checks, no knots, no stubs yet. Driftwood's identity lives in those long dark splits along the grain.
- The laid-down Ferrari trunk is a useful ramp reference but not a log: it has no ends, no taper, and no contact. Better comparisons for form are the mossy fallen log at the bottom right of V09 and your own s0b tree.
Bash: Copy v2: tapered kinked twigs, arc trunk, full rim
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('s0b_fallen.py').read()
a=s.index("TRUNK_POLY = ["); b=s.index("def line_px")
s=s[:a]+'''# trunk: top edge as a smooth convex arc (a few control points, spline-sampled), bottom edge a
# straight diagonal; the root end at the lower left
TOP_CTRL = [(253, 406), (259, 396), (268, 390), (280, 386), (296, 383.5), (320, 382.5), (345, 384),
            (361, 385.5)]
BOT_CTRL = [(361, 388.5), (335, 391), (310, 396), (285, 401), (263, 406)]
# twigs: (polyline, base width).  Width tapers to 1 px at the tip; every vertex is a joint that
# gets a 1-px knob on the lit side; long straight spans are broken into kinks every ~4 px.
TWIGS = [
    ([(313, 382), (314, 376), (316, 371), (316, 366), (317, 362), (316, 358)], 3),
    ([(311, 368), (313, 364), (315, 360)], 2),
    ([(319, 382), (321, 378), (323, 375), (324, 370), (326, 366), (326, 362), (327, 358)], 3),
    ([(328, 372), (329, 375), (333, 378), (335, 383)], 2),
    ([(335, 383), (334, 377), (334, 372), (336, 367), (340, 364)], 2),
    ([(345, 384), (345, 380), (346, 376), (346, 371), (348, 369)], 3),
    ([(359, 385), (359, 378), (360, 372), (359, 368)], 2),
    ([(300, 383), (299, 378), (301, 373), (301, 369), (300, 366)], 3),
    ([(291, 388), (290, 381), (290, 375), (291, 371)], 2),
    ([(296, 384), (294, 379), (285, 377), (276, 378), (270, 379), (264, 382), (257, 384), (250, 385)], 3),
    ([(283, 377), (282, 373), (284, 371), (285, 374)], 2),
    ([(277, 378), (275, 376)], 2),
    ([(272, 380), (272, 385), (271, 391)], 2),
    ([(266, 381), (265, 386), (266, 392)], 2),
    ([(258, 384), (258, 390), (259, 398)], 2),
    ([(252, 383), (251, 390), (253, 398), (251, 401)], 2),
]


def spline(pts, n=200):
    pts = np.array(pts, float)
    t = np.linspace(0, 1, len(pts)); tt = np.linspace(0, 1, n)
    from scipy.interpolate import CubicSpline
    cs = CubicSpline(t, pts, bc_type='natural')
    return cs(tt)


'''+s[b:]
a=s.index("def draw(H, W, snap=True):"); b=s.index("def rim(M):")
s=s[:a]+'''def draw(H, W, snap=True):
    from skimage.draw import polygon as fillpoly
    M = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    top = spline(TOP_CTRL); bot = np.array(BOT_CTRL, float)
    poly = np.concatenate([top, bot])
    rr, cc = fillpoly(poly[:, 1] - Y0, poly[:, 0] - X0, (H, W))
    M[rr, cc] = True
    for tw, w0 in TWIGS:
        segs = list(zip(tw[:-1], tw[1:]))
        total = sum(np.hypot(b[0] - a[0], b[1] - a[1]) for a, b in segs)
        run = 0.0
        for k, (a, b) in enumerate(segs):
            lx, ly = line_px(a, b)
            L = max(len(lx) - 1, 1)
            for j, (x, y) in enumerate(zip(lx, ly)):
                f = (run + j / L * np.hypot(b[0] - a[0], b[1] - a[1])) / max(total, 1e-6)
                w = int(round(w0 - (w0 - 1) * f))
                for dx in range(max(w, 1)):
                    if 0 <= y - Y0 < H and 0 <= x + dx - X0 < W:
                        M[y - Y0, x + dx - X0] = True
            run += np.hypot(b[0] - a[0], b[1] - a[1])
            # joint knob: one pixel on the outside of the bend at each interior vertex
            if k < len(segs) - 1:
                x, y = b
                if 0 <= y - Y0 < H and 0 <= x - 1 - X0 < W:
                    M[int(y) - Y0, int(x) - 1 - X0] = True
    return M


'''+s[b:]
a=s.index("def rim(M):"); b=s.index("def main():")
s=s[:a]+'''def rim(M):
    """the back-light wraps the whole silhouette on the glow side: every mass pixel whose right
    neighbour or upper neighbour is open sky gets the rim -- on twigs that is the right-hand
    pixel ('##r'), on the trunk a run along the top contour."""
    P = np.pad(M, 1)
    right_open = M & ~P[1:-1, 2:]
    up_open = M & ~P[:-2, 1:-1]
    return right_open | up_open


'''+s[b:]
open('s0b_fallen.py','w').write(s)
EOF
cd .. && python3 src/s0b_fallen.py

    # his seams sit inside the form.  Within 2 px of the outline the class is rounded, and along

    nbr_out = np.zeros_like(wood)
    for dy, dx in ((1, 0), (-1, 0), (0, 1), (0, -1)):

    near = wood & ndi.binary_dilation(sil, iterations=1)
    kk = np.clip(np.round(v), 0, 4).astype(int)
    st = np.where(near, tech.CLASS_STEPS[kk], st)
    # majority along the outline (window 7 px), per class
    counts = np.stack([ndi.uniform_filter(((kk == c) & sil).astype(float), 7) for c in range(5)])
    maj = np.argmax(counts, 0)
    st = np.where(sil, tech.CLASS_STEPS[maj], st)

# Driftwood, after Mark Ferrari

Whole trees and logs stranded on river gravel bars: silvered, barkless trunks with root wads and
snapped stubs, piled against each other. Everything here is made by the programs in `src/`; the
stage images are in `stages/`, the parameter sheets in `sheets/`. Photos are in `refs/` (sources
in `refs/sources.tsv`) plus `~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg`.



nice -n 10 python3 src/s0a_pale_trunk.py      # stage 0 copies
nice -n 10 python3 src/s0a2_pipeline_copy.py
nice -n 10 python3 src/s0b_fallen.py
nice -n 10 python3 src/s1_log.py              # stage 1 boards
nice -n 10 python3 src/s1_compare.py




The technique code is `src/geo.py` (world: logs as bent tapering round-cone chains, ends, sphere
tracing, cast shadows), `src/tech.py` (the proven form rule), `src/render.py` (the painter),
`src/gravel.py` (the ground).

---

## Stage 0: copying Ferrari first

Ferrari never painted driftwood. The nearest passages in his work are the pale sunlit trunk in
*Deep Forest* (V09), its grey barked trunk, and the fallen dead tree in the dusk marsh of V02.
I read his indexed files directly (the Canvas Cycle JSON; same loader as the master-copy arm), so
I could see his form classes as numbers rather than guessing them from RGB.

### 0A: the pale trunk (V09), a smooth cylinder in sun

![s0a](stages/s0a_pale_trunk_8x.png)
![s0a2](stages/s0a2_pipeline_copy_8x.png)

Printed as index symbols, the 13-px trunk is **five form classes in a fixed spatial order**,
counted from the shadow edge:



cc 4444 3 3 1 1 2 2        c = cool sky-blue rim   4 = core   3 = halftone   1 = light   2 = lit edge




- In value those are 138 | 125 | 149 | 179 | 166: reflected rim, core, halftone, light, and a lit
  edge that falls back one step as the form turns away. The classical order, and the whole range
  is only 55 grey levels: the mass is held close.
- Between classes he uses a checker of the two neighbours, 1–2 px wide, running along the axis.
  Only three mixing levels occur: pure, 50%, pure. The pattern repeats every 8 rows exactly
  (a DPaint gradient fill).
- **Dithering follows spatial adjacency, not value adjacency.** The core (125) dithers straight
  into the halftone (149), skipping the cool rim colour (138) that lies between them in value.

The first copy (`s0a`) fitted a 1-D profile across his own silhouette: 87% of trunk pixels match
his class exactly. The second (`s0a2`) matters more: it paints the trunk from a lit 3-D cylinder,
with the classes as a function of the angle `d` around the axis measured from the light's peak.
The fitted boundaries are `rim | -69° | core | -42° | halftone | -13° | light | +4° | edge`, and it
matches 89% of his pixels. So the core starts some 20° after the geometric terminator, and the
light class is a thin band at the peak. This is `tech.cylinder_classes`, and because it is
geometric it rotates to lie down.

*The critic's objection, which I accept:* both 0A copies borrow his silhouette and background, so
they only test the ramp, and the one place I had to draw (the limb leaving the trunk at the top)
came out as a blob. That is why I made 0B.

### 0B: the fallen dead tree (V02), drawn by hand

![s0b](stages/s0b_fallen_6x.png)

This time the silhouette is my own sketch: the trunk's top edge as a smooth arc from eight control
points, the bottom edge a straight diagonal, and sixteen twig gestures read off his image. My
rules redraw the strokes. What I found:

- Against the dusk glow the whole body is **one near-black class**, with no form inside the mass.
- The only light is a 1-px **rim** of dull maroon on every silhouette pixel that faces the glow:
  a run along the trunk's top, and on each twig the single right-hand pixel (`##r`).
- Twigs are **2–3 px thick near the trunk and stay thick** until the last third, then taper to 1 px.
  They kink every 3–5 px, have knobs at the joints, and fork in V and Y crooks. Every twig is
  attached to something.
- The trunk contour is runs that shorten smoothly round the curve (14, 10, 6, 5, 4, 4, 4, 3, 3 ...).

In the first version the twigs were thin, even-width straight sticks, some floating free, and the
trunk top was a ruled staircase. The critic caught all three. Silhouette IoU with his mass is now
0.62; the character (bold crooked twigs, a rim on everything) is close, while the placement of
individual twigs is only a sketch. **Not a blind match yet.** His twigs are denser in the left
cluster, and his rim along the trunk top reads more strongly than mine.

---

## Stage 1: one log in greyscale

![compare](stages/s1_compare_6x.png)
![lights flat](stages/s1_lights_flat_4x.png)
![lights gravel](stages/s1_lights_gravel_4x.png)
![ends](stages/s1_ends_5x.png)
![sizes](stages/s1_sizes_4x.png)

**The form.** A log is `geo.make_log`: a chain of seven round cones with a lateral bow, a vertical
sag, a taper of about 0.75 and ±8% lumps in radius. It is lowered until its lowest point touches
the gravel, so a bowed log rests on two contacts and lifts off between them. My first log was a
perfect cylinder with a square cap. The critic called it a steel pipe, and it was.

**Light and shadow families.** `tech.cylinder_classes` gives the five classes; the cast shadow
(ray-marched toward the sun against all logs) forces anything it covers into the shadow family.
What I learned while taking the cylinder from standing to lying:

- **Wood values must be held close, as his are.** With a wide grey ramp (core 84, light 202) a
  lying log lit from above read as a dark pipe with a white stripe, because in Ferrari's scheme
  most of the camera-facing side falls in the core class. With his spacing (core 112, rim 128,
  halftone 152, edge 178, light 204) the same index image reads as pale silvered wood. The palette
  is the light; the index map didn't change.
- **Seams stay 1–2 px wide at every trunk width.** Ferrari's 6-px limbs have pure 1-px bands
  (`c4431 2`), his 13-px trunk mixes over whole classes, and wider forms keep narrow seams rather
  than scaling the checker up. `tech.sharpen` sets the gain to max(13/d, d/13).
- **No dither near the outline.** Within 2 px of the silhouette the class is rounded, and along
  the outline each pixel takes the majority class of its neighbours along the edge. Before this,
  the lit top of a diagonal log was a regular sawtooth of two tones, the fastest mechanical tell
  in the picture (critic, round 2).
- **The sun-side silhouette gets a 1-px light run** where the lit plane meets what is behind it.
  Under back light this is the only light the log gets, which is Ferrari's V02 rim and his c5
  boulder edge.

**Contact.** There is a 1-px darkest line on the ground under the silhouette wherever the ground
point lies within 2 px of the log. Where a bowed log lifts off, the ground under it (a vertical
ray meets the log within 2.5 px) is a black pocket. My first pocket covered every gap under the
log and made a 4-px black band. A larger gap is just ground in shadow.

**Ends** (`s1_ends`):

- *sawn*: the face is one lit plane at the body's light value (not white; that read as chalk),
  with a 1-px darker arris ring, growth rings as broken arcs when it is at least 16 px across,
  and one to three radial checks from near the pith.
- *worn* (water-rounded): no cut; the round cone's cap turns the classes round the end. This is
  the commonest driftwood end, and the one my first version lacked.
- *snapped* and *splinter*: the trunk's shell stands out further in a few angular sectors (the
  teeth); the break face between them falls toward the axis. Value follows relief: tooth tips 7,
  the torn face 6 or 4, crevices at the root 2–3, and orphan pixels are removed. My first attempts
  were a non-Lipschitz SDF (speckle misses) and then thin cones (wires). A profile divided by its
  Lipschitz bound fixed the tracing.

**Sizes** (`s1_sizes`): at a 120-px length the log is 12 px thick and carries all five classes.
Below a projected diameter of 4.5 px the tracer aliases, so `render.thin_logs` draws the axis as
a clean-run line whose rows, from the lit side, are: 1 px → light; 2 → light, core; 3 → light,
halftone, core; 4 → edge, light, halftone, core. The smallest log is a light pixel over a dark one.

**Honest comparison.** Beside the photo the log reads as a pale bent log with a sawn end, sitting
on the ground. Beside Ferrari it is his cylinder language, but smooth: there is no material yet,
and his trunks are never this plasticky. The gravel (stage 5) is still too busy at small scales.
The critic asked for plain ground in the form studies, so every stage-1 board also has a
plain-ground version.

Write: elements/driftwood/src/marks.py
"""marks.py -- the wood's material, drawn as strokes on the log's surface.

Observed (Cliffs_and_driftwood, Driftwood_on_Gravel_bar, braided bar):

  * silvered driftwood is smooth and pale; its grain is a set of long, gently wavy dark lines
    running ALONG the axis, broken into dashes, a few px apart; they bunch toward the silhouette
    because the cylinder turns away (foreshortening);
  * checks are the few long splits along the grain: darker and longer than grain, often with a
    lighter lip on the sun side (the split's upper edge catches the light);
  * knots are eyes: a dark centre, grain lines deflecting around them (bulging past, converging
    behind);
  * fresh bark is the INVERSE: a dark body crossed by a braided network of lighter ridges
    (Ferrari's snow-forest trunks: lit ridge lines interwoven along the axis on dark bark).

Painted (Ferrari: narrow forms are clean 1-px runs; contrast lives inside the mark):

  * every mark is a curve on the 3-D surface -- theta(s) with a low-frequency wobble -- sampled at
    half-pixel spacing, projected, and rasterised to 1-px runs; hidden where the surface faces
    away or something is in front;
  * grain = one value step darker than the pixel's own form class;
  * check = two to three steps darker, with a 1-px lip one step lighter on its sun side;
  * knot = an elongated dark eye with a lit rim on the sun side; nearby grain bends round it;
  * density scales with the projected diameter: no grain below ~7 px, checks only down to ~4 px.
"""
import numpy as np
import geo

# one value step darker / lighter than a wood step (value ramp order 0<1<2<3<4<5<6<7)
DARKER = np.array([0, 0, 1, 2, 2, 4, 5, 6])
DARKER2 = np.array([0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 4])
LIGHTER = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 7])


def surf_point(lg, s, th, r_scale=1.0):
    p, ax, r, (e1, e2) = lg.frame_at(s)
    n = np.cos(th) * e2 + np.sin(th) * e1
    return p + n * r * r_scale, n


def _visible(buf, cam, k, P, N, tol_px=1.2):
    x, y = cam.project(P)
    xi = np.floor(x).astype(int); yi = np.floor(y).astype(int)
    H, W = buf['obj'].shape
    ok = (xi >= 0) & (xi < W) & (yi >= 0) & (yi < H)
    V = -cam.f
    ok &= (N @ V) > 0.05
    xi = np.clip(xi, 0, W - 1); yi = np.clip(yi, 0, H - 1)
    ok &= buf['obj'][yi, xi] == k
    t = (P - cam.look) @ cam.f + 60.0
    ok &= np.abs(buf['t'][yi, xi] - t) < tol_px / cam.scale + 0.02
    return xi, yi, ok


def _curve(lg, s0, s1, th0, wob_amp, wob_len, ph, step_m, knots=()):
    s = np.arange(s0, s1, step_m)
    th = th0 + wob_amp * np.sin(2 * np.pi * s / wob_len + ph) + 0.5 * wob_amp * np.sin(2 * np.pi * s / (wob_len * 0.37) + 2 * ph)
    # knots push grain around them: a smooth bump in theta, away from the knot centre
    for (ks, kth, kr_s, kr_th) in knots:
        dth = np.angle(np.exp(1j * (th - kth)))
        ds = (s - ks) / (kr_s * 2.2)
        w = np.exp(-ds * ds) * np.exp(-(dth / (kr_th * 2.5)) ** 2)
        th = th + np.sign(dth + 1e-6) * w * kr_th * 1.6
    return s, th


def wood_marks(cv, buf, logs, cam, light, sh, weather=1.0, seed=0, mat_ids=(0,)):
    """draw grain, checks and knots onto cv (in place)."""
    V = -cam.f
    lx = light.L @ cam.r; ly = -(light.L @ cam.u)
    sdx = int(np.sign(lx)) if abs(lx) > 0.35 else 0
    sdy = int(np.sign(ly)) if abs(ly) > 0.35 else 0
    H, W = cv.step.shape
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        rng = np.random.default_rng(lg.seed * 7 + seed)
        dpx = 2 * lg.rad.mean() * cam.scale
        if dpx < 4:
            continue
        step_m = 0.45 / cam.scale
        own = (buf['obj'] == k)
        base = cv.step.copy()
        bark = lg.kind == 'bark'
        # ---- knots (placed first; grain bends round them)
        knots = []
        if dpx >= 9:
            for _ in range(rng.integers(0, 3) + (1 if weather > 0.5 else 0)):
                ks = rng.uniform(0.1, 0.9) * lg.L
                kth = rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0)          # mostly on the upper / front half
                r0 = lg.frame_at(ks)[2]
                kr_s = r0 * rng.uniform(0.25, 0.5)
                kr_th = rng.uniform(0.18, 0.3)
                knots.append((ks, kth, kr_s, kr_th))
        # ---- grain
        if dpx >= 7:
            n_lines = int(np.clip(np.pi * dpx / 3.2, 6, 60))
            for i in range(n_lines):
                th0 = (i + rng.uniform(-0.3, 0.3)) / n_lines * 2 * np.pi
                wob = rng.uniform(0.04, 0.12) * (2.5 if bark else 1.0)
                wl = lg.L * rng.uniform(0.3, 0.9)
                ph = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
                s = 0.0
                while s < lg.L:
                    dash = rng.uniform(6, 30) / cam.scale * (1.6 if bark else 1.0)
                    gap = rng.uniform(3, 14) / cam.scale * (0.5 if bark else 1.0) / max(weather, 0.3)
                    ss, th = _curve(lg, s, min(s + dash, lg.L), th0, wob, wl, ph, step_m, knots)
                    s += dash + gap
                    if len(ss) < 2:
                        continue
                    P = np.array([surf_point(lg, a, b)[0] for a, b in zip(ss, th)])
                    N = np.array([surf_point(lg, a, b)[1] for a, b in zip(ss, th)])
                    xi, yi, ok = _visible(buf, cam, k, P, N)
                    xi, yi = xi[ok], yi[ok]
                    if len(xi) == 0:
                        continue
                    if bark:
                        cv.step[yi, xi] = LIGHTER[base[yi, xi]]
                    else:
                        cv.step[yi, xi] = DARKER[base[yi, xi]]
        # ---- checks
        if dpx >= 4 and not bark:
            nchk = rng.integers(1, 3) + int(weather > 0.7)
            for _ in range(nchk):
                th0 = rng.uniform(-1.6, 1.6)
                ln = lg.L * rng.uniform(0.25, 0.7)
                s0 = rng.uniform(0, lg.L - ln)
                ss, th = _curve(lg, s0, s0 + ln, th0, 0.03, lg.L * 0.8, rng.uniform(0, 6), step_m, knots)
                P = np.array([surf_point(lg, a, b)[0] for a, b in zip(ss, th)])
                N = np.array([surf_point(lg, a, b)[1] for a, b in zip(ss, th)])
                xi, yi, ok = _visible(buf, cam, k, P, N)
                xi, yi = xi[ok], yi[ok]
                if len(xi) == 0:
                    continue
                cv.step[yi, xi] = DARKER2[base[yi, xi]]
                # lip on the sun side, only where the surface is lit
                if (sdx or sdy) and not light.overcast:
                    ly2, lx2 = yi + sdy, xi + sdx
                    okl = (ly2 >= 0) & (ly2 < H) & (lx2 >= 0) & (lx2 < W)
                    ly2, lx2 = ly2[okl], lx2[okl]
                    okl = own[ly2, lx2] & (base[ly2, lx2] >= 4)
                    # do not overwrite the check itself
                    chk = np.zeros((H, W), bool); chk[yi, xi] = True
                    okl &= ~chk[ly2, lx2]
                    cv.step[ly2[okl], lx2[okl]] = LIGHTER[base[ly2[okl], lx2[okl]]]
        # ---- knots drawn last
        for (ks, kth, kr_s, kr_th) in knots:
            pts = []
            for a in np.linspace(-1, 1, 9):
                for b in np.linspace(-1, 1, 9):
                    if a * a + b * b <= 1:
                        pts.append((ks + a * kr_s, kth + b * kr_th, a, b))
            P = np.array([surf_point(lg, s_, t_)[0] for s_, t_, _, _ in pts])
            N = np.array([surf_point(lg, s_, t_)[1] for s_, t_, _, _ in pts])
            xi, yi, ok = _visible(buf, cam, k, P, N)
            ab = np.array([(a, b) for _, _, a, b in pts])
            rr = np.hypot(ab[:, 0], ab[:, 1])
            for j in np.nonzero(ok)[0]:
                y, x = yi[j], xi[j]
                if rr[j] < 0.55:
                    cv.step[y, x] = 1 if not bark else 0
                elif rr[j] < 1.0:
                    cv.step[y, x] = DARKER[base[y, x]]
            # lit rim of the knot's swelling on the sun side
            if (sdx or sdy) and not light.overcast:
                cy_, cx_ = yi[ok], xi[ok]
                if len(cy_):
                    cy0, cx0 = int(np.median(cy_)), int(np.median(cx_))
                    for (y, x) in zip(cy_, cx_):
                        if (y - cy0) * sdy + (x - cx0) * sdx >= 1:
                            yy, xx = y + sdy, x + sdx
                            if 0 <= yy < H and 0 <= xx < W and own[yy, xx] and base[yy, xx] >= 4:
                                if cv.step[yy, xx] == base[yy, xx]:
                                    cv.step[yy, xx] = LIGHTER[base[yy, xx]]

cat > s2_material.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 2: the wood's material -- grain, checks, knots; fresh bark for contrast."""
import sys, os, time
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
import numpy as np
import geo, render
from paint import Light
from util import board

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')


def cell(az=300, el=40, W=120, H=64, scale=50, pitch=35, seed=3, ends=('sawn', 'worn'), yaw=12, L=2.2,
         r=0.2, kind='drift', weather=1.0, marks=True, flat=True, look=(0, 0, 0.1), overcast=False, **kw):
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, look=look)
    lg = geo.make_log(L=L, r=r, yaw=yaw, seed=seed, ends=ends, kind=kind, **kw)
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el, overcast=overcast)
    cv, buf, sh = render.render([lg], cam, li, seed=seed, stone_m=0.06,
                                opts=dict(gravel=not flat, marks=marks, weather=weather))
    return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())


if __name__ == '__main__':
    t = time.time()
    P = [('no marks', cell(marks=False)), ('grain + checks + knots', cell()),
         ('seed 7', cell(seed=7)), ('fresh bark (inverse)', cell(kind='bark'))]
    board(P, scale=5, cols=2, title='s2 material').save(f'{ROOT}/scratch/s2_try.png')
    print(time.time() - t)
EOF

def boards():
    from PIL import Image
    from util import fit_photo
    from s0a_pale_trunk import load
    d = load('V09'); grey = d['pal'][d['idx'][60:200, 452:486]]
    grey = np.rot90(grey, 1)
    sf = np.array(Image.open(os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/shared/refs/ferrari_snow_forest_boulders.webp')).convert('RGB'))
    sf = sf[::2, ::2][150:330, 400:480]
    sf = np.rot90(sf, 1)[:, :140]
    ph = fit_photo(f'{ROOT}/refs/commons_Cliffs_and_driftwood_at_St_ngehuvud_b_jpg_10933a5d.jpg', (150, 540, 1100, 730), (140, 28))
    ph2 = fit_photo(f'{ROOT}/refs/openverse_Driftwood_on_Gravel_bar_540722fe.jpg', (250, 180, 850, 768), (70, 69))

    b = cell(W=140, H=56, kind='bark', look=(0, 0, 0.1))
    board([('photo (Stangehuvud, /7)', ph), ('Ferrari V09 grey trunk, laid down', grey),
           ('mine: silvered driftwood', a), ('mine: fresh bark (inverse)', b),
           ('Ferrari snow-forest bark, laid down', sf)], scale=5, cols=1,
          title='s2 material at 5x').save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s2_compare_5x.png')
    P = []
    for sd in (3, 7, 11, 4):
        for w, lab in ((0.4, 'light weathering'), (1.0, 'heavy weathering')):
            P.append((f'seed {sd}, {lab}', cell(seed=sd, weather=w)))

    board([('8x detail, 90 px/m', a)], scale=8).save(f'{ROOT}/stages/s2_detail_8x.png')


if __name__ == '__main__' and len(sys.argv) > 1:
    boards()

# output:
## Round 4 — 2026-09-25 22:00 — stage 2 `stages/s2_compare_5x.png`, `s2_detail_8x.png`, `s2_material_4x.png`, `s2_bark_5x.png`
**REVISE, with real progress.** The grain is in: at 5× "mine" reads as silvered wood at a glance, which the stage-1 tube never did. (My read; your call. Stage 1 still had open notes, including the spiral end and the dashed rim.)

What gives it away (`s2_detail_8x.png`):

1. **Every grain stroke is parallel to the log axis and flat-ended.** At 8× the grain is stacked rectangular runs, all at exactly the log's slope. That reads as brushed metal or stacked planks. In the Stangehuvud photo (top of `s2_compare_5x`) the grain *waves*: it swells around the knot and the dark hollow at the right, converges where the wood narrows, and ends in tapering points, not square ends.
2. **The main check is a ruled staircase.** The long black split near the bottom runs the full length in equal 6–8 px steps. A check in weathered wood opens and closes, wanders, and stops partway.
3. **The silhouette is still a smooth tube with a flat, vertical cut end** (with a 1 px white line on the end). The photo's piece is gnarled: the outline bulges at the knots, a limb stub breaks the top, and the end is split and rounded. Grain on a perfect cylinder can't carry "driftwood" alone. The contour has to say it too.
4. No knots or hollows. The photo's single most identifying feature is the dark knot hole with grain flowing round it. One knot with grain deflecting round it would do more than any amount of straight grain.
5. The ground is plain now. Good; it lets the log speak.

## Round 3 — 2026-09-25 21:46 — stage 1 `stages/s1_ends_5x.png`, `s1_lights_gravel_4x.png`, `s1_lights_flat_4x.png`, `s1_sizes_4x.png`, `s1_compare_8x.png`
**Stage 1: REVISE.** Real progress: the log bends now, tapers, and has ends of different kinds, and the back-lit cast shadows ("back-left 30", "back-right 25") are convincing. (My read; your call. s0b's round-2 notes still stand.)

What still keeps it from reading as driftwood:

1. **Three parallel stripes, end to end.** Every panel is a lit band, then a 50% checker band, a mid band, and a dark bottom row, running the full length with no change. That reads as a tube, a horn, or a banana (`s1_compare_8x.png`, "mine"). In the photo beside it, the light on the log is broken by *grain*: long dark checks and splits following the fibres, lighter raised ridges between them, and the value shifting where the log swells or dips. Stage 1 is greyscale, but the grain is form: checks are grooves, and they're what make wood look like wood.
2. **The sawn end is a spiral glyph.** "Sawn end, 60 px/m" draws the rings as a spiral, which is the cartoon-log icon. Weathered driftwood end grain is a flat, lighter face with a few dark radial cracks (checks radiating from the heart) and a softened rim. No rings are visible at this scale.
3. **Snapped and splinter ends are pixel litter.** A few isolated white and black pixels with no structure. A splintered end

# output:
V = -cam.f
    obj = buf['obj']
    wood = obj >= 0
    # ---------- wood form: five classes around the axis
    d, phL = tech.normal_plane_angles(buf['N'], V, light.L, buf['T'])
    v = tech.cylinder_classes(d, phL, sh=sh)
    if light.overcast:
        # overcast: the sky is the light; peak straight up
        d2, ph2 = tech.normal_plane_angles(buf['N'], V, np.array([0, 0, 1.0]), buf['T'])
        v = tech.cylinder_classes(d2, ph2)
    diam = 2 * buf['rad'] * cam.scale
    # with marks on, big logs turn their form by MARK DENSITY, not by checker: the classes are
    # quantized nearly pure (seams at most 1 px) and the grain supplies the gradation
    if o.get('marks', True):
        v = tech.sharpen(v, diam, max_gain=12.0)
        v = np.where(diam >= 7, tech.sharpen(v, np.full_like(diam, 1.0), max_gain=12.0), v)
    else:
        v = tech.sharpen(v, diam)
    st, k = tech.quantize_classes(v)
    if o.get('marks', True):
        pure = tech.CLASS_STEPS[np.clip(np.round(v), 0, 4).astype(int)]
        st = np.where(diam >= 7, pure, st)
    # near the silhouette there is no dither: Ferrari's outlines are clean runs of ONE tone and
    # his seams sit inside the form.  Within 2 px of the outline the class is rounded, and along
    # the outline itself each pixel takes the majority class of the outline pixels around it
    # (so a diagonal lit edge is one tone, not a sawtooth of two).
    from scipy import ndimage as ndi
    nbr_out = np.zeros_like(wood)
    for dy, dx in ((1, 0), (-1, 0), (0, 1), (0, -1)):
        nbr_out |= np.roll(np.roll(obj, dy, 0), dx, 1) != obj
    sil = wood & nbr_out
    near = wood & ndi.binary_dilation(sil, iterations=1)
    kk = np.clip(np.round(v), 0, 4).astype(int)
    st = np.where(near, tech.CLASS_STEPS[kk], st)
    # majority along the outline (window 7 px), per class
    counts = np.stack([ndi.uniform_filter(((kk == c) & sil).astype(float), 7) for c in range(5)])
    maj = np.argmax(counts, 0)
    st = np.where(sil, tech.CLASS_STEPS[maj], st)
    part = buf['part']
    # sun-side silhouette: where the lit plane meets what is behind it, a 1-px light run
    # (contre-jour: this is the only light the log gets -- Ferrari's V02 rim, c5 boulder edge)
    if not light.overcast:
        lx = light.L @ cam.r; ly = -(light.L @ cam.u)
        sdx = int(np.sign(lx)) if abs(lx) > 0.35 else 0
        sdy = int(np.sign(ly)) if abs(ly) > 0.35 else 0
        if sdx or sdy:
            nb = np.roll(np.roll(obj, -sdy, 0), -sdx, 1)
            tn = np.roll(np.roll(buf['t'], -sdy, 0), -sdx, 1)
            edge = wood & ((nb != obj) & ((nb < 0) | (tn > buf['t'] + 0.05)))
            ndl0 = (buf['N'] * light.L).sum(-1)
            rimlit = edge & (ndl0 > -0.05) & ~sh
            st = np.where(rimlit, np.maximum(st, 6), st)
    # ---------- end faces
    st = end_faces(st, buf, logs, light, sh, cam)
    cv.mat[wood] = WOOD
    for k, lg in enumerate(logs):
        if lg.kind == 'bark':
            cv.mat[obj == k] = BARK
    cv.step[wood

Bash: Add splinter slivers and clean-line helper
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
r=open('render.py').read()
r=r.replace("                if r_px >= 8:\n                    # growth rings","                if r_px >= 20:\n                    # growth rings")
